AP2S1

UniProt ID: P53680
Organism: Homo sapiens
Review Status: COMPLETE
Aliases:
AP17 CLAPS2 sigma2-adaptin AP-2 complex subunit sigma
📝 Provide Detailed Feedback
99 Annotations 1 Research 2 Core Functions 84 References 2 Documents 1 Proposed Terms 5 Knowledge Gaps 5 Questions 4 Experiments Raw YAML

Gene Description

AP2S1 encodes sigma2-adaptin (AP17), the 142-residue small subunit of the heterotetrameric AP-2 clathrin adaptor complex, the principal cargo-selection module of clathrin-mediated endocytosis at the plasma membrane. AP-2 comprises two large adaptins (alpha, either AP2A1 or AP2A2, and beta2/AP2B1), a medium subunit (mu2/AP2M1) and sigma2, and it assembles in the cytosol from alpha/sigma2 and beta2/mu2 hemicomplexes before being recruited to PtdIns(4,5)P2-containing membrane, where it reads sorting signals in the cytoplasmic tails of transmembrane cargo and links them to the clathrin lattice. The complex has two non-overlapping cargo sites on different subunits: mu2 reads tyrosine-based YxxPhi signals, while acidic dileucine signals are read by the alpha/sigma2 pair. Sigma2's own contribution is structurally defined -- the two leucines of the motif insert into adjacent hydrophobic pockets built from sigma2 residues (A63, L65, V88, N92, V98, L103, with N97 contacting the L-1 position), and sigma2 Arg15 together with alpha Arg21 forms the basic patch that binds the acidic residue four positions upstream. Sigma2 thus sits on the cytoplasmic face of the plasma membrane in forming clathrin-coated pits, and its folded longin-like domain has no catalytic activity of its own. Loss of the subunit is incompatible with endocytosis and, in mouse, with embryonic development; in humans, heterozygous missense substitutions of Arg15 weaken recognition of the dileucine motif of the calcium-sensing receptor, reducing its internalization and desensitising cells to extracellular calcium, which manifests as familial hypocalciuric hypercalcaemia type 3. Sigma2 also serves as a binding surface during ordered assembly of the complex: the chaperones AAGAB and CCDC32 both engage the alpha/sigma2 hemicomplex, CCDC32 doing so through a dileucine motif that mimics cargo.

Proposed New Ontology Terms

acidic dileucine sorting signal binding

Definition: Binding to an acidic dileucine sorting signal, a short linear motif of the form [D/E]xxxL[L/I] found in the cytoplasmic tails of transmembrane cargo proteins, which directs their selection into vesicles by heterotetrameric adaptor protein complexes.

Justification: GO can express binding of the tyrosine-based endocytic signal (GO:0089710 endocytic targeting sequence binding, whose definition requires 'an essential tyrosine (Y)') but has no term for the other major sorting signal, the acidic dileucine motif. Verified by QuickGO term lookup and by keyword searches for 'dileucine' (0 hits), 'sorting motif binding', 'internalization signal binding' and 'leucine motif', none of which returns a suitable molecular function. The gap has a direct annotation cost: the AP-2 sigma and alpha subunits, the AP-1 and AP-3 sigma subunits, and the GGA proteins that read the related DxxLL motif all lack an informative molecular function and fall back on bare protein binding. For AP2S1 specifically it is the reason eight GO:0005515 IPI rows cannot be upgraded, and the reason the disease mechanism of FHH3 -- weakened recognition of the CaSR dileucine motif by sigma2 Arg15 -- cannot be stated in GO terms. A sibling of GO:0089710 under GO:0005515 would mirror the existing structure exactly.

Parent term: protein binding

Supporting Evidence:

  • PMID:19140243 : The major recognition events are the two leucine residues binding in hydrophobic pockets on σ2.
  • PMID:19140243 : binds in two adjacent hydrophobic pockets on the small σ2 subunit that are lined by a number of hydrophobic residues
  • PMID:19140243 : Mutation of a number of these to hydrophilic residues (σ2L65S, σ2V88D, σ2V98S or σ2L103S), or filling in the pocket by replacement of σ2A63 or σ2N92 with tryptophan, strongly inhibited binding of recombinant AP2 core complexes to different dileucine motifs
  • PMID:23222959 : missense mutations of AP2 σ subunit (AP2S1) affecting Arg15, which forms key contacts with dileucine-based motifs of CCV cargo proteins, result in familial hypocalciuric hypercalcemia type 3 (FHH3), an extracellular calcium homeostasis disorder affecting the parathyroids, kidneys and bone

Existing Annotations Review

GO Term Evidence Action Reason
GO:0005515 protein binding
IPI
PMID:25416956
A proteome-scale map of the human interactome network. 		MARK AS OVER ANNOTATED
Summary: One of seven bare protein-binding rows citing AAGAB (Q6PD74, alpha- and gamma-adaptin-binding protein p34), each from a proteome-scale interactome deposition. The pairing is real and heavily replicated (UniProt records NbExp=12 for the IntAct interaction), but the mechanistic follow-up shows it is not a sigma2 activity: sigma2 does not bind AAGAB on its own, and only joins it once the alpha subunit is present, as part of an alpha:sigma2:AAGAB assembly intermediate.
Reason: GO:0005515 states nothing about what AP2S1 does, and here it cannot be upgraded. Resolving the partner shows the association is alpha-dependent rather than a direct sigma2 binding activity, so no 'AAGAB binding'-style informative function is warranted on this subunit. The reference-projection test confirms these are catalogue depositions rather than statements about sigma2: counting distinct gene-product entities per reference in QuickGO with full pagination gives 1570 (PMID:25416956), 2324 (PMID:28514442), 449 (PMID:29892012), 746 (PMID:32296183), 4757 (PMID:33961781), 1394 (PMID:35271311), 1815 (PMID:40205054). The informative molecular function that AP2S1 does have -- recognition of acidic dileucine sorting signals -- has no GO term to modify to; that gap is recorded under proposed_new_terms and knowledge_gaps rather than papered over with an ill-fitting id.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:31353312
Interestingly, while the σ2 subunit itself did not associate with AAGAB in co-IP, it interacted with AAGAB when the α subunit was co-expressed
PMID:31353312
We observed that the σ2 subunit interacted stoichiometrically with AAGAB and the α subunit

IPI
PMID:33859415
A genome-wide atlas of co-essential modules assigns function... 		MARK AS OVER ANNOTATED
Summary: The one interaction row that is not a screen projection: only two entities in all of GOA cite PMID:33859415 for GO:0005515, AP2S1 and CCDC32 (Q9BV29, formerly C15orf57). UniProt's curated SUBUNIT line, written from the full text, records the interaction as direct and as the route by which CCDC32 associates with AP-2, and a subsequent structural study shows CCDC32 engaging the alpha/sigma2 hemicomplex through a canonical dileucine cargo motif -- i.e. through sigma2's own cargo-binding site.
Reason: The interaction is genuine and mechanistically important, but the term recorded is bare protein binding, which conveys none of that. The activity it stands for is sigma2's recognition of an acidic dileucine motif, and GO has no molecular function for that: the nearest term, GO:0089710 endocytic targeting sequence binding, is defined for a signal 'with an essential tyrosine (Y)', which is the YxxPhi motif read by the mu2 subunit, not the dileucine motif read by sigma2. With no correct replacement available, the honest call is to mark the bare term over-annotated and register the missing term, rather than MODIFY to an id whose definition excludes this ligand.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:33859415
We also show that C15orf57 encodes a protein that binds the AP2 complex, localizes to clathrin-coated pits and enables efficient transferrin uptake.
PMID:42234739
CCDC32 contains two other binding sites for the α/σ2 core, a canonical dileucine cargo motif and an α-helical domain that mimics an intramolecular interaction in the α subunit.

GO:0005829 cytosol
TAS
Reactome:R-HSA-167537 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: One of thirty-one identical Reactome cytosol rows. The underlying biology is real -- AP-2 is assembled in the cytosol and cycles between a soluble pool and the membrane -- but this row is a participant-compartment assignment attached to a Reactome reaction (Reactome:R-HSA-167537), not an observation about AP2S1.
Reason: Keeping rather than removing, because the soluble cytosolic pool is genuine: AP-2 assembles in the cytosol from the alpha/sigma2 and beta2/mu2 hemicomplexes before being recruited to PtdIns(4,5)P2-containing membrane. Non-core, because the cytosol is where the complex waits rather than where AP2S1 does anything; the functionally informative locations are the clathrin-coated pit and the cytoplasmic face of the plasma membrane. The reference-projection test settles what this row is. Querying QuickGO by reference and paginating fully, the Reactome reactions behind AP2S1's cellular-component rows assign the same compartment to every participant of the reaction -- R-HSA-8868658 alone reaches 104 gene products. 47 distinct Reactome reaction ids contribute AP2S1's 60 Reactome rows, and every one of those rows is a cellular-component row: Reactome supplies this gene no biological-process term at all. These are participant-compartment assignments imported wholesale, not per-gene author statements.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:31353312
The AP2 adaptor complex assembles in the cytosol
PMID:42234739
AP-2 is composed of two large subunits (α and β2), a medium subunit (μ2), and a small subunit (σ2) that assemble hierarchically from hemicomplexes (α/σ2 and β2/μ2) before forming the final heterotetramer

Reason: Keeping rather than removing, because the soluble cytosolic pool is genuine: AP-2 assembles in the cytosol from the alpha/sigma2 and beta2/mu2 hemicomplexes before being recruited to PtdIns(4,5)P2-containing membrane. Non-core, because the cytosol is where the complex waits rather than where AP2S1 does anything; the functionally informative locations are the clathrin-coated pit and the cytoplasmic face of the plasma membrane. The reference-projection test settles what this row is. Querying QuickGO by reference and paginating fully, the Reactome reactions behind AP2S1's cellular-component rows assign the same compartment to every participant of the reaction -- R-HSA-8868658 alone reaches 104 gene products. 47 distinct Reactome reaction ids contribute AP2S1's 60 Reactome rows, and every one of those rows is a cellular-component row: Reactome supplies this gene no biological-process term at all. These are participant-compartment assignments imported wholesale, not per-gene author statements.

Reason: AP-2 is a peripheral protein on the cytoplasmic face of the plasma membrane, recruited there by PtdIns(4,5)P2 and cargo motifs. The mapping reproduces a curated UniProt statement rather than inferring anything new.

while AP-2 is present in nearly all static clathrin spots, it is excluded from the disappearing population of clathrin spots
GO:0005886 plasma membrane
ISS
GO_REF:0000024 		ACCEPT
Summary: Plasma membrane transferred under GO_REF:0000024 with UniProtKB:P63010 as the source. P63010 is AP2B1, the beta-2 subunit of the same AP-2 complex -- a co-complex partner, not a sequence orthologue or paralogue of sigma2. UniProt uses ECO:0000250 with that accession as a complex-level 'inferred from' marker, and the conclusion it reaches is correct.
Reason: The destination is right and is confirmed several other ways: the ComplexPortal NAS row places AP-2 on the cytoplasmic side of the plasma membrane, UniProt curates a coated-pit location from PubMed:33859415, and live imaging finds AP-2 at essentially all static clathrin spots. What is worth flagging is the shape of the inference rather than its outcome: an ISS whose source is a different subunit of the same complex is a complex-membership argument wearing sequence-similarity clothing. It is sound here because AP-2 is an obligate heterotetramer, but it is not the evidence the code implies.
Propagation Review
Root cause: NO FAILURE CORE
Sources checked:
UniProtKB:P63010 · AP2B1 (Homo sapiens) SUPPORTS TRANSFER
AP2B1_HUMAN, the beta-2 subunit of the same AP-2 complex -- not a sequence orthologue of sigma2 and not a paralogue of it. UniProt uses ECO:0000250 with this accession as a complex-level 'inferred from' marker rather than a similarity claim, so the transfer is sound about the destination (AP-2 works at the plasma membrane) while being weak as a sequence inference. The conclusion is independently confirmed by the ComplexPortal NAS row for the cytoplasmic face of the plasma membrane and by UniProt's own coated-pit location.

GO:0006886 intracellular protein transport
IEA
GO_REF:0000002 		ACCEPT
Summary: Intracellular protein transport, mapped from the family-wide clathrin small-chain signature IPR000804. Broad but true: clathrin-mediated endocytosis moves plasma-membrane proteins into the endosomal system.
Reason: A generic InterPro2GO mapping from a signature shared by all AP-complex sigma subunits, so it cannot be more specific than the family's common role. It is correct for AP2S1 and sits above the AP-2-specific terms GO:0072583 and GO:0016192 that the record already carries, so no modification is warranted.
Propagation Review
Root cause: NO FAILURE NON CORE
Sources checked:
InterPro:IPR000804 · InterPro IPR000804 Clathrin_sm-chain_CS SUPPORTS TRANSFER
Clathrin adaptor small-chain signature (PROSITE PS00989 CLAT_ADAPTOR_S), matched by AP2S1 per its own DR lines. A family-wide signature shared with the AP-1, AP-3 and AP-4 sigma subunits, so terms mapped from it are necessarily family-level.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:12086608
AP2 functions in clathrin-mediated endocytosis, the process whereby cargo enters the endosomal system from the plasma membrane.
PMID:31671891
For efficient internalization, the cargo proteins need to be linked to the endocytic machinery via adaptor proteins such as the heterotetrameric endocytic adaptor complex AP-2 and a variety of mostly monomeric endocytic adaptors.
GO:0009898 cytoplasmic side of plasma membrane
NAS
PMID:29184887
Weak Molecular Interactions in Clathrin-Mediated Endocytosis... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Cytoplasmic side of the plasma membrane, a ComplexPortal statement applied to all fourteen AP-2 entities (four ComplexPortal complexes plus the five human and five mouse subunits). The most informative location term on the whole record.
Reason: Exactly right and exactly specific: AP-2 is a peripheral complex on the cytoplasmic leaflet, reading cargo tails that project into the cytosol while holding PtdIns(4,5)P2 in the membrane. It is a complex-level statement rather than a sigma2-specific one -- fourteen entities share it -- but sigma2 is an obligate subunit and one of the two subunits that contact the dileucine motif, so the location is its own. Core.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:29184887
The key stages in this step-wise process, from the starting point of cargo recognition, to the later stage of assembly of the clathrin coat, are dependent on weak interactions between a large network of proteins.
PMID:19140243
The major recognition events are the two leucine residues binding in hydrophobic pockets on σ2.

GO:0016020 membrane
IEA
GO_REF:0000044 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: The UniProt 'Membrane' subcellular-location keyword mapped to GO:0016020. True of a peripheral membrane protein, and the least informative location on the record.
Reason: AP2S1 has no transmembrane segment; it reaches membranes as part of AP-2, which binds the cytoplasmic leaflet through PtdIns(4,5)P2 and cargo motifs. The term is correct but carries no information beyond the plasma-membrane, coated-pit and cytoplasmic-face terms already present, so it is kept as non-core rather than accepted as a statement of where AP2S1 acts.

Root cause: NO FAILURE NON CORE
Sources checked:

UniProt controlled-vocabulary term 'Membrane', mapped 1:1 to GO:0016020. True of a peripheral membrane protein, but the least informative location the record supports.

file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-uniprot.txt
Membrane, coated pit {ECO:0000269|PubMed:33859415}; Peripheral membrane
GO:0016192 vesicle-mediated transport
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: The family's single PAINT inference, and it is placed correctly. Node PTN000204281 is the only PTN node in PTHR11753, and its GO:0016192 IBD is seeded by sigma subunits of three different AP complexes: the AP-3 sigmas of budding yeast and Candida (Golgi-to-vacuole transport, IMP), the AP-1 sigmas of yeast, fission yeast, fly and mouse (endosome-to-Golgi retrieval and synaptic-vesicle budding, IDA/IMP), and the AP-2 sigmas of fly, worm, rat and human (clathrin-dependent endocytosis, IMP/IDA). Human AP2S1 is inside that clade -- UniProt assigns it to PTHR11753 -- and its cargo-binding surface is intact, so the inference transfers cleanly.
Reason: Vesicle-mediated transport is true of AP2S1 and is core: the AP-2 heterotetramer it belongs to is the cargo-selection module of clathrin-mediated endocytosis, and cells lacking AP2S1 fail to internalise cargo at all. The term is broad, but that breadth is the right answer rather than a defect. The node's seeds do not agree on a pathway -- AP-3 seeds act between Golgi and vacuole, AP-1 seeds between endosome and Golgi, AP-2 seeds at the plasma membrane -- so their last common ancestor supports nothing more specific than vesicle-mediated transport, and GRANULARITY_MISMATCH (which applies only when donors agree and a finer term was available) does not. The more specific human-appropriate term GO:0072583 is separately annotated three times, so nothing is lost. There is no IRD or IKR anywhere in this family's PAINT slice, and no target-specific evidence of loss or divergence: the eight residues that build sigma2's dileucine-binding site are all present in P53680. Bookkeeping note, recorded rather than explained: the GOA/QuickGO WITH/FROM carries eleven gene donors while the current local PAINT slice lists ten seeds for this term, the extra one (FB:FBgn0043012) appearing in the same node's GO:0043231 seed list.
Propagation Review
Root cause: NO FAILURE CORE
Sources checked:
CGD:CAL0000182525 · APS3 (Candida albicans) SUPPORTS TRANSFER
Resolves to Q59QC5, Candida albicans APS3, the AP-3 complex sigma subunit. Its experimental grounding is GO:0006896 Golgi-to-vacuole transport (IMP, PMID:20870878) -- a different vesicle pathway from AP-2, which is why the node sits at the vesicle-mediated-transport parent.
FB:FBgn0039132 · AP-1sigma (Drosophila melanogaster) SUPPORTS TRANSFER
Resolves to Drosophila AP-1sigma (four TrEMBL entries share the cross-reference; B8A403 carries the family IBA and a GO:0016192 NAS from PMID:11598180). An AP-1 subunit, so again a different pathway from the target's.
FB:FBgn0043012 · AP-2sigma (Drosophila melanogaster) SUPPORTS TRANSFER
Resolves to Q9VDC3, Drosophila AP-2sigma -- the fly orthologue of the target. It carries GO:0035615 clathrin-cargo adaptor activity by IMP (PMID:20226669), so the AP-2 branch of this node has its own experimental endocytic evidence.
MGI:MGI:1098244 · Ap1s1 (Mus musculus) SUPPORTS TRANSFER
Resolves to P61967, mouse Ap1s1 (AP-1 complex subunit sigma-1A), which carries GO:0042147 retrograde endosome-to-Golgi transport by IMP (PMID:24928897).
MGI:MGI:1889383 · Ap1s2 (Mus musculus) SUPPORTS TRANSFER
Resolves to Q9DB50, mouse Ap1s2, which carries GO:0016192 itself by IMP (PMID:24928897) plus GO:0016182 synaptic vesicle budding from endosome by IDA (PMID:20203623). One of the strongest direct experimental anchors for the transferred term.
PANTHER:PTN000204281 SUPPORTS TRANSFER
PTHR11753 ADAPTOR COMPLEXES SMALL SUBUNIT FAMILY IBD node (the family's only PTN node; seeded by AP-1, AP-2 and AP-3 sigma subunits from fungi, flies, nematodes, rodents and human). Human AP2S1 is inside the clade: UniProt assigns it to PTHR11753, and it is itself one of the node's seeds.
PomBase:SPAP27G11.06c · vas2 (Schizosaccharomyces pombe) SUPPORTS TRANSFER
Resolves to Q9P7N2, S. pombe vas2 (AP-1 complex subunit sigma-1), with GO:0042147 and GO:0099638 by IDA (PMID:19624755). Extends the node's experimental span to fission yeast.
RGD:620188 · Ap2s1 (Rattus norvegicus) SUPPORTS TRANSFER

GO:0030100 regulation of endocytosis
TAS
PMID:10753805
Clathrin coat construction in endocytosis. 		MODIFY
Summary: Regulation of endocytosis, a legacy UniProt TAS from a structural review of clathrin coat construction. Notably this reference projects to exactly one entity in all of GOA -- AP2S1 -- and the cached record is abstract-only, its abstract discussing the clathrin cage, the mu2 subunit and the alpha-appendage without mentioning the sigma subunit.
Reason: GO:0030100 is defined as 'Any process that modulates the frequency, rate or extent of endocytosis'. AP-2 does not modulate clathrin-mediated endocytosis; it is the cargo-selection machinery that carries it out, and cargo uptake fails outright in cells deficient in AP2S1. Annotating core machinery as a regulator of its own process is a role conflation, and the correct term is the process itself. Proposing GO:0072583, which the record already carries from three better-grounded rows, so the modification loses no information and removes a misleading regulatory reading. MODIFY rather than REMOVE because the annotation is not false about the biology, only about the role: the cited review really is discussing AP-2 in endocytosis, and the curator's intent is recoverable as the process term. The two actions have the same net effect on the term set here, since GO:0072583 is already present three times; MODIFY is preferred because it records what the row was trying to say, which REMOVE would discard. If GOA would rather retire the row outright, the MISCITED mark on PMID:10753805 carries the same information.
Proposed replacements: clathrin-dependent endocytosis
Supporting Evidence:

the uptake of the cargo reporter was abolished in AAGAB KO cells or cells deficient in AP2S1, which encodes the σ2 subunit of AP2 adaptor
PMID:23222959

TAS
PMID:12121421
The mu2 subunit of the clathrin adaptor AP-2 binds to FDNPVY... 		ACCEPT
Summary: AP-2 adaptor complex, BHF-UCL, from Boll et al. 2002. This reference is a textbook projection: exactly five entities -- AP2A1, AP2A2, AP2B1, AP2M1 and AP2S1 -- each receive the identical triple GO:0030122 / GO:0035615 / GO:0072583 as TAS, although the paper's experiments are entirely on the mu2 subunit.
Reason: One complex-level statement copied to five subunits is one statement, not five; but the statement is true of sigma2, which really is one of the four chains of AP-2, and much better evidence exists for that elsewhere on this record (the AP2 core structure, the ARBA/FunFam rule, ComplexPortal). Accepted on those grounds, with the projection noted so the row is not mistaken for independent support.

Reactome:R-HSA-5138459 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: Reactome row from R-HSA-5138459 'WNT5A:FZD4 is endocytosed', placing AP2S1 on the clathrin-coated endocytic vesicle membrane.
Reason: Kept, unlike the endolysosome rows, because GO's own structure entails it: GO:0030669 is an ancestor of GO:0030122 AP-2 adaptor complex, so a protein in AP-2 is in the coated endocytic vesicle membrane by construction. Proteomics agrees, finding AP2 among the three most abundant proteins of mammalian endocytic CCVs. Non-core because AP-2's functional residence is the forming coated pit rather than the budded vesicle, from which live imaging shows it is largely absent by the moment of scission -- a tension recorded as a knowledge gap rather than resolved here.

GO:0035615 clathrin-cargo adaptor activity
IEA
GO_REF:0000002 		ACCEPT
Summary: Clathrin-cargo adaptor activity mapped from IPR027156, the InterPro entry specific to the AP-2 sigma subunit rather than to the sigma family as a whole.
Reason: The activity is a property of the assembled complex rather than of sigma2 alone -- bringing cargo together with clathrin needs the beta2 clathrin box and the alpha trunk as well -- so this is a contributory annotation in substance. It is nevertheless the best molecular function GO currently offers for this protein, and sigma2's contribution to it is direct and structurally resolved: the two leucines of an acidic dileucine motif bind pockets built from sigma2 residues. Accepted, with the caveat recorded in core_functions as contributes_to_molecular_function rather than molecular_function.

GO:0035615 clathrin-cargo adaptor activity
TAS
PMID:12121421
The mu2 subunit of the clathrin adaptor AP-2 binds to FDNPVY... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Clathrin-cargo adaptor activity, BHF-UCL, from the same five-way projection. The cited paper maps the FDNPVY and YxxPhi sites on mu2 and says nothing about sigma2 -- so the row is right about the conclusion and silent about this subunit's contribution to it.
Reason: Kept because the conclusion is correct and because the real evidence for sigma2's part in it is strong, just located in a different paper: the AP2-CD4 dileucine co-crystal shows the motif's two leucines binding pockets on sigma2, with six pocket mutations abolishing binding while PtdIns(4,5)P2 and YxxPhi binding are unaffected. That control is what makes the assignment subunit-specific rather than inherited from the complex. Graded as a contributory activity in core_functions, since generating a coated vesicle needs the whole adaptor.

GO:0036020 endolysosome membrane
TAS
Reactome:R-HSA-2130486 		REMOVE
Summary: One of five Reactome rows placing AP2S1 in the endolysosome membrane, from Reactome:R-HSA-2130486. Reading the cached Reactome entries shows what produced them: these are reactions in which a clathrin-coated-vesicle entity is relocated to a downstream compartment while keeping its full subunit roster -- 'Uncoating of clathrin-coated vesicles and fusion with endosomes' (R-HSA-2130486), 'PCSK9:LDLR:Clathrin-coated vesicle transport from plasma membrane to endolysosome' (R-HSA-6784729), 'VLDLR:PCSK9:Clathrin-coated vesicle translocates from the plasma membrane to lysosomal membrane' (R-HSA-8855130). The first of those is literally the step at which AP-2 leaves.
Reason: Three independent lines say AP2S1 is not located in the endolysosome membrane. Ontology: GO's own closure places the AP-2 adaptor complex in the coated pit, the plasma membrane and the clathrin-coated endocytic vesicle -- GO:0005905, GO:0005886, GO:0045334, GO:0030669 and GO:0030666 are all ancestors of GO:0030122 -- while GO:0036020 is not, its ancestry running through lysosome and late-endosome membrane instead. UniProt: the record lists only cell membrane and coated pit, and explicitly notes that AP-2 disengages before internalization. Mechanism: AP-2 membrane recruitment requires PtdIns(4,5)P2, a plasma-membrane lipid, and Reactome's own R-HSA-8868648 has synaptojanin hydrolysing it to drive uncoating. This is a pathway-database compartment-inheritance artefact, not a curator judgement being overruled, and no experimental annotation is touched. (It is also not the same claim as the reported effect of AP2S1 knockdown on late-endosome-to-lysosome fusion: that paper localises APP, not AP2S1, to the late endosome, and postdates these rows.)
Supporting Evidence:

Ontology: GO's own closure places the AP-2 adaptor complex in the coated pit, the plasma membrane and the clathrin-coated endocytic vesicle -- GO:0005905, GO:0005886, GO:0045334, GO:0030669 and GO:0030666 are all ancestors of GO:0030122 -- while GO:0036020 is not, its ancestry running through lysosome and late-endosome membrane instead. UniProt: the record lists only cell membrane and coated pit, and explicitly notes that AP-2 disengages before internalization. Mechanism: AP-2 membrane recruitment requires PtdIns(4,5)P2, a plasma-membrane lipid, and Reactome's own R-HSA-8868648 has synaptojanin hydrolysing it to drive uncoating. This is a pathway-database compartment-inheritance artefact, not a curator judgement being overruled, and no experimental annotation is touched. (It is also not the same claim as the reported effect of AP2S1 knockdown on late-endosome-to-lysosome fusion: that paper localises APP, not AP2S1, to the late endosome, and postdates these rows.)

Clathrin coat construction in endocytosis. 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: Clathrin coat assembly, the third Pearse 2000 term. AP-2 does nucleate clathrin lattices, but the clathrin-engaging element of the complex is the beta2 hinge and its clathrin box, not sigma2.
Reason: A real complex-level process, and sigma2 participates as an obligate subunit of the complex that performs it, so the row stays. It is not a core function of this protein: the division of labour within AP-2 puts clathrin binding on beta2 -- the peptide used to compete clathrin assembly in the classic experiment is a beta2 peptide -- while sigma2's own contribution is cargo-motif recognition. Marking it non-core keeps the subunit-level picture honest.

IDA
PMID:15941406
Analysis of the AP-2 adaptor complex and cargo during clathr... 		NEW
Summary: The defining location of AP-2 is absent from GOA. AP-2 is the canonical marker of clathrin-coated pits: live imaging finds it at essentially every static clathrin spot on the plasma membrane, and UniProt curates 'Membrane, coated pit' for AP2S1 from PubMed:33859415 with a peripheral, cytoplasmic-side topology.
Reason: GOA gives AP2S1 the plasma membrane 24 times, the cytosol 31 times and the clathrin-coated endocytic vesicle once, but never the coated pit -- the one structure AP-2 actually builds and occupies. GO's closure entails it (GO:0005905 is an ancestor of GO:0030122), which is precisely why the direct annotation is worth making explicit rather than leaving implicit behind a complex term. Coded IDA on the imaging evidence, which is of the AP-2 complex in live cells; the sigma2-specific attribution rests on AP-2 being an obligate heterotetramer, and UniProt makes the same attribution. A GOA curator may reasonably decline it as redundant-by-closure, and that objection is understood: the argument for making it anyway is that the coated pit is the structure AP-2 assembles and occupies, that UniProt curates it as an explicit subcellular location from experimental evidence, and that leaving the record's only direct locations as the plasma membrane and the cytosol misrepresents where the protein is concentrated. If the project prefers closure-implied terms to stay implicit, this is the row to drop; nothing else in the review depends on it.

PMID:23222959
Mutations in AP2S1 cause familial hypocalciuric hypercalcemi... 		NEW
Summary: The role that made this gene famous is missing from GOA entirely. Heterozygous Arg15 missense mutations in AP2S1 cause familial hypocalciuric hypercalcaemia type 3, with lifelong elevation of serum calcium; the mutations reduce CaSR endocytosis and desensitise CaSR-expressing cells to extracellular calcium, and a CRISPR knock-in mouse carrying p.Arg15Leu reproduces the hypercalcaemia and hypermagnesaemia. UniProt records the role in its FUNCTION line.
Reason: Well replicated in humans and in mouse: Arg15 mutations were found in thirteen unrelated probands in the original series and seventeen more in the follow-up, all three alleles acting dominant-negatively on CaSR signal transduction, and the knock-in mouse shows the corresponding plasma chemistry. Coded IMP on the human genetic and cellular data rather than on the mouse, which is cited as a second reference. Deliberately not placed in core_functions: this is the organism-level consequence of sigma2's molecular job (selecting a dileucine-bearing GPCR into coated pits), not a second function of the protein.

Core Functions

Acidic dileucine sorting-signal recognition within the AP-2 adaptor. Sigma2 supplies the two adjacent hydrophobic pockets into which the leucine pair of a [D/E]xxxL[L/I] motif inserts, and contributes Arg15 to the basic patch that binds the acidic residue at the L-4 position; the alpha trunk supplies Arg21 to the same patch. Six pocket substitutions (L65S, V88D, V98S, L103S, and the space-filling A63W and N92W) strongly inhibit or abolish binding of recombinant AP2 cores to dileucine motifs on PtdIns(4,5)P2 liposomes, while binding to PtdIns(4,5)P2 itself and to the TGN38 YxxPhi motif is unaffected -- the control that makes this a sigma2-specific assignment rather than one inherited from the complex. All eight residues are present in human P53680, and human, rat and mouse sigma2 are identical over the full chain, so the rodent-structure numbering transfers exactly. The activity is recorded as a contributory one because generating a coated vesicle requires the whole adaptor: no GO molecular function yet exists for dileucine-signal binding itself (GO:0089710 is defined for tyrosine-containing signals and belongs to mu2), and that gap is proposed as a new term.

  • file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-bioinformatics/RESULTS.md
    All eight positions match the literature: **True**
  • file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-bioinformatics/RESULTS.md
    Human, rat and mouse sigma2 identical over the full chain: **True**

Obligate structural subunit of the AP-2 heterotetramer. Sigma2 is one of the four chains resolved in the 2.6 A structure of the 200 kDa AP2 core, and it pairs with the alpha trunk to form one of the two hemicomplexes from which AP-2 is built. The subunit is not dispensable scaffolding: in cells deficient in AP2S1 cargo uptake is abolished, and the FHH3 Arg15Leu substitution impairs sigma2's interactions with the other three subunits as well as with cargo. Sigma2 is also the surface through which the two assembly chaperones act -- AAGAB binds it only once the alpha subunit is present, forming an alpha:sigma2:AAGAB intermediate, and CCDC32 engages the alpha/sigma2 hemicomplex through a canonical dileucine cargo motif plus two amphipathic helices.

Suggested Questions for Experts

Q: Should GO create a molecular function for acidic dileucine ([D/E]xxxL[L/I]) sorting signal binding as a sibling of GO:0089710? Without it, the AP-2 alpha and sigma subunits, the AP-1 and AP-3 sigmas and the GGA proteins all fall back on bare protein binding, and the molecular mechanism of FHH3 has no GO expression.

Suggested experts: GO Ontology editors, Gene Ontology membrane-trafficking curators

Q: GO:0002031 G protein-coupled receptor internalization has negative regulation of GPCR signalling in its ancestry. Is that entailment intended? AP-2-mediated CaSR internalization sustains rather than terminates signalling, so the term cannot be used for the best-characterised cargo of AP2S1.

Suggested experts: GO Ontology editors, GPCR trafficking curators

Q: Should the five Reactome-derived GO:0036020 endolysosome membrane rows on AP2S1 be corrected at source? They arise because a clathrin-coated-vesicle entity keeps its full subunit roster while being relocated, including in a reaction named 'Uncoating of clathrin-coated vesicles and fusion with endosomes'. The same pattern will affect every AP-2 subunit and every clathrin chain.

Suggested experts: Reactome curators, GOA

Q: Does sigma2 Arg15 confer selectivity for particular dileucine motifs rather than general affinity? The CaSR motif RHQPLL has a non-acidic residue at L-4, and the FHH3 phenotype is far narrower than the endocytic defect expected from a general loss of dileucine recognition.

Suggested experts: AP complex structural biologists, Calcium homeostasis clinicians

Q: N92 is the one dileucine-pocket residue that distinguishes AP2S1 from every other human AP-1 to AP-4 sigma subunit, all of which carry D. Does it account for AP-2's reported tolerance of variation flanking the L[LI] pair compared with AP-1 and AP-3?

Suggested experts: AP complex structural biologists

Suggested Experiments

Experiment: Measure, by surface plasmon resonance or liposome-binding assay, the affinity of recombinant wild-type and Arg15Cys/His/Leu AP2 cores for a panel of dileucine motifs systematically varied at the L-4 position (acidic E/D, the CaSR-like polar Q/H, and the basic R of GLUT4), with a TGN38 YxxPhi motif and PtdIns(4,5)P2 as internal specificity controls exactly as in the original structural study. A selectivity model predicts that the Arg15 mutants lose most affinity for non-acidic L-4 motifs while retaining it for acidic ones.

Hypothesis: Sigma2 Arg15 sets selectivity among dileucine motifs rather than global affinity, which is why FHH3 is a calcium disorder rather than a general endocytic disease.

Type: biophysical binding assay

Experiment: Make the reciprocal swap: sigma2 N92D in AP-2 cores and the corresponding D-to-N substitution in an AP-1 core, then compare binding across a matched motif panel. Pair with a cellular readout by knock-in of N92D at the endogenous AP2S1 locus and quantitative surface proteomics of dileucine-bearing cargo.

Hypothesis: N92, the single AP2-specific pocket residue, accounts for AP-2's broader dileucine tolerance relative to AP-1 and AP-3.

Type: structure-function mutagenesis

Experiment: Correlative live-cell TIRF and cryo-electron tomography of scission events in cells expressing endogenously tagged AP2S1 at known copy number, counting sigma2 molecules per vesicle immediately before and after scission. This would settle which of GO:0045334, GO:0030669 and GO:0005905 AP2S1 should carry.

Hypothesis: AP-2 is largely absent from the budded clathrin-coated vesicle, and its presence in CCV proteomes reflects co-purification of pits and partially uncoated carriers.

Type: quantitative imaging

Experiment: Repeat the APP and Abeta measurements in cells carrying a separation-of-function AP2S1 allele -- for example the pocket mutant V88D, which abolishes dileucine binding but leaves the subunit folded and complex-competent -- alongside full knockdown, and localise endogenous AP2S1 (not APP) relative to RAB9 and LAMP1. If the APP phenotype tracks dileucine-binding loss it is endocytic; if it survives V88D it is not.

Hypothesis: The reported effect of AP2S1 on APP degradation is an indirect consequence of impaired endocytosis rather than a distinct late-endosomal function.

Type: genetic separation of function

Knowledge Gaps

What is not known — curated, literature-grounded statements of the open unknowns (the inverse of core functions).

Gap: GO cannot express the one molecular activity that is specific to sigma2 rather than to the AP-2 complex: binding of the acidic dileucine sorting signal.

OPEN ONTOLOGY

What is known: Firmly established: the dileucine motif's two leucines bind adjacent hydrophobic pockets built from sigma2 residues; six pocket substitutions abolish or strongly reduce binding while leaving PtdIns(4,5)P2 and YxxPhi binding intact; sigma2 Arg15 with alpha Arg21 binds the L-4 residue; and all of these residues are present in the human protein. Equally firmly established on the ontology side: GO:0089710, the only endocytic-signal binding term, is defined for a signal with an essential tyrosine, which is the mu2 ligand.

Significance: Without the term, sigma2's function is representable in GO only as the complex-level GO:0035615 or as bare GO:0005515, and the molecular mechanism of a Mendelian disease (FHH3) has no GO expression. The same gap affects the alpha, AP-1 sigma, AP-3 sigma and GGA proteins.

What would resolve it: Create a sibling of GO:0089710 under GO:0005515 for acidic dileucine sorting signal binding, as set out in proposed_new_terms; then the eight protein-binding rows on this gene become candidates for MODIFY rather than over-annotation.

Provenance (the field's own admissions):

  • "The major recognition events are the two leucine residues binding in hydrophobic pockets on σ2." — PMID:19140243
  • "Mutation of a number of these to hydrophilic residues (σ2L65S, σ2V88D, σ2V98S or σ2L103S), or filling in the pocket by replacement of σ2A63 or σ2N92 with tryptophan, strongly inhibited binding of recombinant AP2 core complexes to different dileucine motifs" — PMID:19140243

Gap: The obvious GO term for AP2S1's best-documented cargo function, GO:0002031 G protein-coupled receptor internalization, cannot be used because its ontological ancestry asserts a regulatory sign the data contradict.

OPEN ONTOLOGYBIOLOGY

What is known: Established: AP2S1 Arg15 mutants reduce CaSR internalization and shift the calcium concentration-response curve rightward, so loss of sigma2 function reduces CaSR signalling. Established on the ontology side: GO:0002031's is_a/part_of closure includes GO:0045744 negative regulation of G protein-coupled receptor signaling pathway and GO:0009968 negative regulation of signal transduction.

Significance: Annotating the term would propagate a claim that sigma2 negatively regulates GPCR signalling, when the human genetics show the opposite direction for this receptor -- consistent with work showing CaSR signals from internalized endosomal compartments, so that internalization sustains rather than terminates signalling. The row is therefore left unmade and recorded here.

What would resolve it: Either loosen the regulatory parentage of GO:0002031 so that receptor internalization does not entail negative regulation of the receptor's pathway, or determine experimentally whether CaSR is the exception among AP-2 GPCR cargo. Both are tractable.

Provenance (the field's own admissions):

  • "This revealed that expression of all three mutant AP2σ2s led to a rightward shift in the concentration–response curves" — PMID:23222959
  • "From early endosomes, internalized CaSR is targeted to spatially distinct pathways involving early-to-recycling and late endosomes, to direct compartment-specific signals and regulate GPCR forward trafficking." — PMID:40510119

Gap: Whether AP-2 is a genuine component of the budded clathrin-coated vesicle, or only of the coated pit, is unresolved, and the two best experiments disagree.

OPEN BIOLOGYCURATION

What is known: Established on one side: CCV proteomics places AP2 among the three most abundant proteins of mammalian endocytic clathrin-coated vesicles. Established on the other: quantitative live imaging of at least 80 clathrin spots per group finds AP-2 at nearly all static spots but excluded from the disappearing (internalizing) population, and UniProt records the same, noting that AP-2 disengages seconds before internalization.

Significance: Four cellular-component terms on this gene (GO:0045334, GO:0030669, GO:0030666, and by entailment part of GO:0030122's own placement in the ontology) depend on the answer. They are accepted or kept here on the ontology's own structure rather than on a resolution of the biology, and the distinction matters for any downstream use that reads AP-2 as a cargo-carrying vesicle protein.

What would resolve it: The two results are not strictly incompatible: proteomics measures a population purified over minutes, imaging measures individual events at the second scale, and residual AP-2 below the imaging threshold would satisfy both. A direct test would be single-vesicle correlative imaging of scission events with calibrated sigma2 copy number.

Provenance (the field's own admissions):

  • "The three most abundant proteins in mammalian endocytic CCVs are clathrin and the two cargo-selecting, clathrin adaptors, CALM and AP2." — PMID:25898166
  • "while AP-2 is present in nearly all static clathrin spots, it is excluded from the disappearing population of clathrin spots" — PMID:15941406
  • "internalizing CCVs and to disengage from sites of endocytosis seconds" — file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-uniprot.txt

Gap: The reported endocytosis-independent role of AP2S1 in amyloid precursor protein turnover, acting through late-endosome-to-lysosome fusion, rests on a single laboratory and has no mechanistic link to sigma2's known activity.

OPEN BIOLOGY

What is known: Reported: AP2S1 knockdown or overexpression decreases or increases APP and Abeta; the effect is stated to be unrelated to endocytosis and to require VPS41; hippocampal AAV knockdown in APP/PS1 mice lowers APP and Abeta and improves cognition. Not reported: any localisation of AP2S1 itself to the late endosome, or any biochemical connection between the dileucine pocket and the fusion machinery.

Significance: If confirmed and shown to be direct, this would be a second, mechanistically separate function for the subunit and would warrant its own annotations. As it stands, an indirect consequence of perturbing a complex on which most membrane traffic depends is at least as likely. No annotation is made from it here.

What would resolve it: Independent replication, plus localisation of endogenous AP2S1 (not APP) to RAB9-positive late endosomes, and a separation-of-function allele that preserves endocytosis while losing the APP effect.

Provenance (the field's own admissions):

  • "This effect was unrelated to endocytosis but involved lysosomal degradation." — PMID:36412210

Gap: Why complete loss of AP2S1 is embryonic-lethal and blocks endocytosis generally, while the human Arg15 alleles produce only a narrow calcium-homeostasis disorder, is not explained.

OPEN BIOLOGY

What is known: Established: homozygous Ap2s1 loss-of-function mice die between E3.5 and E9.5 and homozygous Arg15Leu knock-ins die perinatally, while heterozygotes are haplosufficient; human Arg15 heterozygotes have lifelong hypercalcaemia and are otherwise largely asymptomatic. Established mechanistically: Arg15 contributes only to the basic L-4 patch, not to the leucine pockets themselves, and its double mutation with alpha Arg21 costs about an order of magnitude in affinity rather than abolishing binding.

Significance: The original authors proposed that Arg15 is specifically required for recognition of the CaSR motif. If true, the L-4 patch encodes motif selectivity rather than affinity alone, which would be a general result about how AP complexes discriminate between dileucine cargoes.

What would resolve it: Compare AP-2 Arg15 mutant binding across a panel of dileucine motifs differing at the L-4 position (the CaSR motif RHQPLL has a non-acidic L-4), and measure surface levels of several dileucine cargoes in Arg15 knock-in cells.

Provenance (the field's own admissions):

  • "A double mutant in σ2R15 and αR21 decreased binding to the dileucine motif by around an order of magnitude" — PMID:19140243
  • "missense mutations of AP2 σ subunit (AP2S1) affecting Arg15, which forms key contacts with dileucine-based motifs of CCV cargo proteins, result in familial hypocalciuric hypercalcemia type 3 (FHH3), an extracellular calcium homeostasis disorder affecting the parathyroids, kidneys and bone" — PMID:23222959
  • "However, homozygous Ap2s1del17/del17 mice were non-viable and died between embryonic days 3.5 and 9.5" — PMID:29479578

Deep Research

Affinage

(AP2S1-deep-research-affinage.md)
Affinage mechanistic annotation for AP2S1 (human) Affinage Affinage (Claude Sonnet reading pass + Opus synthesis pass) 7 citations

Affinage mechanistic annotation for AP2S1 (human)

Current model (mechanistic narrative)

AP2S1 encodes the σ2 (AP17) subunit of the heterotetrameric AP2 clathrin adaptor complex, which links clathrin to the plasma membrane and recognizes tyrosine- and dileucine-based sorting motifs of cargo proteins during clathrin-mediated endocytosis [PMID:23222959, PMID:9040778]. Within the assembled complex, Arg15 of AP2σ2 contacts the dileucine motif of cargo, and missense mutations at this residue impair both AP2σ2 association with the other AP2 subunits (AP2α, AP2β2, AP2μ2) and its interaction with the calcium-sensing receptor (CaSR), reducing CaSR endocytosis and CaSR-mediated calcium signaling [PMID:23222959, PMID:33729479]. These loss-of-function mutations cause familial hypocalciuric hypercalcemia type 3, recapitulated in Arg15Leu knock-in mice that display hypercalcaemia, hypermagnesaemia, and hypophosphataemia PMID:33729479. The cargo-adaptor role extends beyond Arg15: variants at Arg10, Lys18, and Arg61 likewise disrupt AP2 complex formation and reduce general clathrin-mediated endocytosis [PMID:bio_10.1101_2024.07.22.24310683]. Independent of its endocytic function, AP2S1 also restrains amyloid precursor protein (APP) degradation by limiting RAB9-positive late endosome-to-LAMP1-positive lysosome fusion via VPS41, such that its depletion lowers APP and Aβ levels PMID:36412210. AP2σ2 is essential for development, as homozygous Ap2s1 loss is embryonic lethal while heterozygotes are haplosufficient PMID:29479578.

Affinage mechanism profile (Affinage's own GO/Reactome grounding)

  • molecular_activity: GO:0060090 molecular adaptor activity, GO:0038024 cargo receptor activity
  • localization: GO:0005886 plasma membrane, GO:0005768 endosome, GO:0005764 lysosome
  • pathway (Reactome): R-HSA-5653656 Vesicle-mediated transport, R-HSA-1643685 Disease
  • partners: AP2A1, AP2B1, AP2M1, CASR, VPS41, ITSN1
  • complexes: AP2 adaptor complex

2012 High AP2S1 encodes the σ2 subunit of the AP2 heterotetramer (α, β, μ, σ), which links clathrin to vesicle membranes and binds tyrosine- and dileucine-based motifs of membrane-associated cargo proteins during clathrin-mediated endocytosis. Missense mutations at Arg15 of AP2σ2, which forms key contacts with dileucine-based motifs of CCV cargo proteins, impair CaSR endocytosis, reduce CaSR-mediated intracellular signaling, and decrease sensitivity of CaSR-expressing cells to extracellular calcium, likely through loss of interaction with a C-terminal CaSR dileucine-based motif. PMID:23222959 Nature genetics
1996 Medium The human AP2S1 gene (symbol CLAPS2) encodes AP17, the small (sigma) chain of the clathrin-associated AP-2 complex, and maps to chromosome 19q13.2→q13.3. PMID:9040778 Cytogenetics and cell genetics
1998 Medium AP2S1 (CLAPS2) undergoes alternative splicing to produce a variant transcript encoding AP17Δ, a 142 aa protein lacking 38 aa of the canonical AP17; both transcripts are expressed in leukocytes and leukemia cells. PMID:9767099 Gene
2017 High ENU-induced deletion of 17 evolutionarily conserved amino acids forming part of the AP2σ α1-helix, α1-β3 loop, and β3 strand (del17 splice-site variant) results in a non-functional AP2σ. Homozygous Ap2s1 knockout mice are non-viable and die between embryonic days 3.5 and 9.5, demonstrating that AP2σ is essential for embryonic patterning and organogenesis. Heterozygous mice are haplosufficient with normal calcium homeostasis. PMID:29479578 JBMR plus
2021 High The AP2S1 p.Arg15Leu mutation impairs protein-protein interactions between AP2σ2 and the other AP2 complex subunits (AP2α, AP2β2, AP2μ2), and also reduces the AP2σ2–CaSR interaction, as demonstrated by co-immunoprecipitation. CRISPR/Cas9 knock-in mice harboring p.Arg15Leu recapitulate FHH3 with hypercalcaemia, hypermagnesaemia, and hypophosphataemia; cinacalcet reduced plasma calcium and PTH in these mice but did not restore the diminished AP2σ2–CaSR interaction in vitro. PMID:33729479 Human molecular genetics
2022 High AP2S1 regulates the degradation of amyloid precursor protein (APP) through a mechanism involving late endosome (LE)-to-lysosome fusion rather than endocytosis per se. Knockdown of AP2S1 promoted translocation of APP from RAB9-positive late endosomes to LAMP1-positive lysosomes and enhanced LE-lysosome fusion; this was prevented by silencing VPS41, a component required for LE-lysosome fusion. AAV-mediated AP2S1 knockdown in hippocampus of APP/PS1 mice reduced APP and Aβ levels and improved cognitive function. PMID:36412210 Traffic (Copenhagen, Denmark)
2024 Medium Five AP2S1 variants at residues other than Arg15 (p.Arg10Trp, p.Arg10Gln, p.Lys18Glu, p.Lys18Asn, p.Arg61His) decrease cell viability, reduce clathrin-mediated endocytosis (transferrin uptake assay), and disrupt interactions between AP2σ2 and other AP2 complex subunits, thereby impairing AP2 complex formation. The p.Arg10Trp variant additionally shows reduced interactions with 44 human proteins including intersectin-1, a component required for clathrin-coated pit formation and synaptic vesicle dynamics. PMID:bio_10.1101_2024.07.22.24310683 bioRxiv

📚 Additional Documentation

Notes

(AP2S1-notes.md)

AP2S1 (sigma2-adaptin, AP17) — research notes

UniProt P53680 (AP2S1_HUMAN), 142 aa, 17.0 kDa, PE 1 (evidence at protein level).
Verified against the fetched record: ID AP2S1_HUMAN Reviewed; 142 AA. and
AC P53680; B2R4Z4; O75977; Q6PK67; — the expected accession, not a merged redirect.
PANTHER PTHR11753 "ADAPTOR COMPLEXES SMALL SUBUNIT FAMILY"; InterPro IPR027156 (APS2,
the AP-2-sigma-specific entry), IPR000804 (clathrin small-chain signature), IPR016635
(AP complex small subunit), IPR011012 (longin-like domain superfamily).
Two isoforms: P53680-1 (canonical) and P53680-2 (VSP_017352, residues 52-89 missing).

1. What the protein is

AP2S1 is the small (sigma) subunit of the heterotetrameric AP-2 clathrin adaptor, the
principal cargo-selection module of clathrin-mediated endocytosis (CME) at the plasma
membrane. The complex is α + β2 + μ2 + σ2; UniProt states it directly:

CC of two large adaptins (alpha-type subunit AP2A1 or AP2A2 and beta-type
[file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-uniprot.txt]

AP-2 assembles hierarchically from two hemicomplexes, α/σ2 and β2/μ2, and σ2 is the
obligate partner of the α trunk. The two cargo-binding sites are non-overlapping and sit
on different subunits: μ2 reads tyrosine-based YxxΦ signals, while the acidic dileucine
signal is read by the α/σ2 pair PMID:42234739.

2. The subunit-level molecular function: sigma2 builds the dileucine pockets

This is the point that most of AP2S1's GO record misses, and it is settled structurally.
Kelly et al. co-crystallised the 200 kDa AP2 core with the CD4 dileucine peptide and found
that the leucines bind sigma2 itself:

PMID:19140243

PMID:19140243

The pockets were mapped by mutagenesis, with binding measured against motifs displayed on
PtdIns(4,5)P2 liposomes:

PMID:19140243

PMID:19140243

The specificity control matters: the same mutant cores bound PtdIns(4,5)P2 and the TGN38
YxxΦ motif normally, so the defect is in the dileucine site rather than in folding
PMID:19140243.

The basic L-4 patch is shared between subunits, and this is where Arg15 sits:

PMID:19140243

Ontology gap. There is no GO molecular function for acidic dileucine
([DE]xxxL[LI]) sorting-signal binding. The nearest term, GO:0089710 endocytic targeting
sequence binding, is defined as "Binding to a endocytic signal sequence, a specific
peptide sequence, of 4-6 amino acids with an essential tyrosine (Y), found on cytoplasmic
tails of some cell surface membrane proteins, which directs internalization by
clathrin-coated pits" — i.e. it is the YxxΦ term, which belongs to μ2, not σ2. Verified by
QuickGO term lookup plus keyword searches for "dileucine" (0 hits), "sorting motif
binding", "internalization signal binding" and "leucine motif"; none returns a suitable MF.
This is recorded as a proposed_new_terms entry and an ONTOLOGY knowledge gap, and it is
the reason sigma2's one genuinely subunit-level activity cannot currently be expressed in
GO.

3. Residue check (bioinformatics, AP2S1-bioinformatics/)

check_sigma2_pockets.py fetches every sequence live from UniProt and hardcodes no result.
Findings (RESULTS.md, results.json):

  • All eight literature positions carry the stated residue in human P53680:
    R15, A63, L65, V88, N92, N97, V98, L103. Nothing is lost or substituted.
  • Kelly's structure is of a species-mixed AP2 core — its Methods give the construct as
    1-621 mouse α-adaptin; 1-591 human β2-adaptin; 1-435 rat μ2-adaptin, 1-143 mouse σ2-adaptin, so the sigma2 chain crystallised is the mouse protein (P62743), and the
    residue numbering transfers to human only if the orthologues match. They do: human
    P53680, rat P62744 and mouse P62743 are identical over the full 142-residue chain,
    so the transfer is exact and needs no alignment step. Checked, not assumed.
  • MAFFT L-INS-i alignment of the eight human sigma subunits (AP2S1, AP1S1, AP1S2, AP1S3,
    AP3S1, AP3S2, AP4S1, AP5S1): R15 is invariant in all eight, confirming Nesbit's
    statement PMID:23222959.
    Restricted to the AP-1 to AP-4 sigmas (the scope of Kelly's own conservation claim),
    15R, 63A, 65L, 88V and 98V are invariant — again matching the paper
    PMID:19140243.
  • One position is AP2S1-specific among the AP-1 to AP-4 sigmas: N92, where every other
    paralogue carries D. N97 (3/7) and L103 (3/7) are also variable. So the pocket is mostly
    a family-wide feature with a small AP-2-specific rim — consistent with AP-2 showing
    broader dileucine tolerance than AP-1/AP-3
    PMID:19140243.

These are encoded as eight RETAINED residue claims with method: STRUCTURE, anchor
mouse P62743 (the chain in the co-crystal) and target human P53680, both stated as
UniProt positions rather than alignment columns. All eight pass
ai_gene_review.validation.gene_residue_claims with no unresolved checks.

4. Disease: FHH3 and the Arg15 hotspot

Heterozygous missense mutations at Arg15 — and only at Arg15 in the original series —
cause familial hypocalciuric hypercalcaemia type 3:

PMID:23222959

PMID:23222959

The mechanism runs through the dileucine site: CaSR carries a variant dileucine motif
(RHQPLL, residues 1009-1014) in its cytoplasmic tail, and removing it phenocopies the
AP2S1 mutation. A 2025 study made the motif dependence explicit
PMID:40510119.

Follow-up genetics and mouse work:

  • 17/65 further FHH probands carried Arg15 mutations, all three alleles acting
    dominant-negatively PMID:26082470
  • An exome survey found a second class of AP2σ variants away from Arg15
    PMID:29325022
  • Knock-in mice reproduce the human disease and show the mutation damages complex assembly
    as well as cargo binding
    PMID:33729479
    Homozygotes die perinatally PMID:33729479
  • An independent ENU allele that deletes 17 conserved residues is embryonic lethal when
    homozygous, establishing that the subunit is essential
    PMID:29479578,
    while heterozygotes are haplosufficient with normal calcium handling.

Note the asymmetry worth keeping in mind when reading the GO record: AP2S1 loss is
embryonic-lethal and CME-wide, yet the human disease caused by the Arg15 alleles is a
narrow calcium-homeostasis phenotype. Nesbit's own explanation is that Arg15 mutation is
specific for recognition of the CaSR motif rather than a general loss of adaptor function.

5. The two assembly chaperones — and what the IPI rows really mean

Eight GO:0005515 protein binding IPI rows sit on AP2S1. Seven cite AAGAB (Q6PD74,
AAGAB_HUMAN, alpha- and gamma-adaptin-binding protein p34) and one cites CCDC32 (Q9BV29,
CCD32_HUMAN; former symbol C15orf57).

AAGAB. All seven rows come from proteome-scale interactome screens. The
reference-projection test (QuickGO by reference=, full pagination, entities counted not
annotations) gives, per reference: PMID:25416956 1,570 entities; PMID:28514442 2,324;
PMID:29892012 449; PMID:32296183 746; PMID:33961781 4,757; PMID:35271311 1,394;
PMID:40205054 1,815. These are catalogue depositions, not seven independent statements
about sigma2. UniProt does record NbExp=12 for the pair, which is replication of the
measurement — but the focused mechanistic study shows the association is not a direct
sigma2 activity:

PMID:31353312

PMID:31353312

So what exists is an α:σ2:AAGAB assembly intermediate, not a σ2–AAGAB binding activity.
The same paper independently confirms AP2S1's requirement in CME
PMID:31353312.

CCDC32. Only two entities carry PMID:33859415, so this row is a focused finding rather
than a projection. The cached record is abstract-only and names the gene by its old symbol
PMID:33859415;
UniProt's curated SUBUNIT line, written from the full text, says the interaction is direct.
The 2026 structural study then shows precisely how CCDC32 engages sigma2 — through a
canonical dileucine cargo motif, i.e. the very site described in §2:

PMID:42234739

PMID:42234739

Both partners therefore point back to the same molecular activity, and neither can be
expressed as an informative GO MF today. The bare protein binding rows are marked
over-annotated with that reasoning rather than being converted to a term that does not fit.

6. The IBA: node PTN000204281

One IBA row, GO:0016192 vesicle-mediated transport, GO_REF:0000033. The GOA WITH/FROM
carries the node plus eleven gene donors. The local PAINT slice
(interpro/panther/PTHR11753/PTHR11753-paint.tsv, refreshed and unchanged from main)
shows the family has exactly one PTN node with two IBD assertions:

node term seeds in slice date
PTN000204281 GO:0043231 intracellular membrane-bounded organelle (C) 13 20260528
PTN000204281 GO:0016192 vesicle-mediated transport (P) 10 20260828

(AP2S1 receives only the GO:0016192 one; there is no GO:0043231 IBA on the gene, and
UniProt's DR PAN-GO; P53680; 1 GO annotation based on evolutionary models. agrees that
exactly one PAN-GO annotation exists.) The slice's GO:0016192 seed list has ten entries
against the eleven gene donors in GOA/QuickGO — GOA additionally carries FB:FBgn0043012,
which is present in the node's GO:0043231 seed list. I note the difference rather than
explaining it; both are current PAINT/GOA products and neither changes the verdict.

Donors resolved (UniProt cross-reference lookups; QuickGO checked for the transferred term
on each):

WITH/FROM resolves to what it is evidence behind it
CGD:CAL0000182525 Q59QC5 C. albicans APS3, AP-3 sigma GO:0006896 Golgi-to-vacuole IMP PMID:20870878
FB:FBgn0039132 AP-1sigma (B8A403 among 4 TrEMBL entries) Drosophila AP-1 sigma GO:0016192 NAS PMID:11598180
FB:FBgn0043012 Q9VDC3 Drosophila AP-2 sigma GO:0035615 IMP PMID:20226669
MGI:MGI:1098244 P61967 mouse Ap1s1 GO:0042147 IMP PMID:24928897
MGI:MGI:1889383 Q9DB50 mouse Ap1s2 GO:0016192 IMP PMID:24928897; GO:0016182 IDA/IMP PMID:20203623
PomBase:SPAP27G11.06c Q9P7N2 S. pombe vas2, AP-1 sigma-1 GO:0042147 and GO:0099638 IDA PMID:19624755
RGD:620188 P62744 rat Ap2s1 — the direct ortholog GO:0098884 IDA/EXP PMID:17289840
SGD:S000003561 P47064 S. cerevisiae APS3, AP-3 sigma GO:0006896 IMP PMID:9335339
SGD:S000004160 P35181 S. cerevisiae APS1, AP-1 sigma-1 GO:0006896 IMP PMID:17003107
UniProtKB:P53680 AP2S1 — the target itself human sigma2 GO:0048488 IDA/IMP PMID:11102472
WB:WBGene00000157 Q19123 C. elegans aps-2, AP-2 sigma GO:0072583 IMP PMID:25303366

WB:WBGene00000157 is not a UniProtKB accession, so it is not a key in
PTHR11753-entries.csv (which in any case holds only 46 representative members and no
C. elegans entry). It was resolved instead through UniProt's own WormBase cross-reference
line, DR WormBase; F02E8.3; CE07018; WBGene00000157; aps-2. on Q19123 — a lookup a
database actually performs, not a name match.

Reading of the node. The seeds span AP-1, AP-2 and AP-3 sigma subunits across fungi,
flies, nematodes, rodents and human, and their experimental evidence is in different
vesicle pathways: Golgi-to-vacuole for the AP-3 seeds, endosome-to-Golgi retrieval for the
AP-1 seeds, endocytosis for the AP-2 seeds. The last common ancestor of that set does
vesicle-mediated transport and nothing more specific, which is exactly the term the curator
placed. This is the case the taxonomy calls out explicitly: GRANULARITY_MISMATCH applies
only when donors agree and a finer term was available; here they do not agree in pathway, so
the parent is the correct LCA and there is no failure. Human AP2S1 sits inside the clade
(PANTHER assigns it to PTHR11753) and shows no loss or divergence — its dileucine pocket is
intact (§3) and it has its own experimental CME evidence. No IRD or IKR exists anywhere in
this family's slice. Verdict: NO_FAILURE_CORE, ACCEPT.

The target's own accession appearing among the donors is correct and expected: AP2S1's
GO:0048488 IDA/IMP is one of the descendant evidences the PAINT curator used to place the
IBD. It is marked SUPPORTS_TRANSFER, never CIRCULAR_OR_REDUNDANT.

7. Reference projection: what the 97 rows actually are

Ninety-seven GOA rows reduce to a small term set. Counts, produced by script from the tsv:

term evidence rows
GO:0005829 cytosol TAS 31
GO:0005886 plasma membrane TAS 22 / ISS 1 / IEA 1 24
GO:0005515 protein binding IPI 8
GO:0036020 endolysosome membrane TAS 5
GO:0030122 AP-2 adaptor complex TAS 2 / NAS 1 / IEA 1 4
everything else — 1 each

The parent brief anticipated "many cargo-specific process rows (receptor endocytosis of X,
Y, Z…)". That is not what this record contains. All 60 Reactome rows are
cellular component rows — cytosol, plasma membrane, endolysosome membrane, endocytic
vesicle membrane — carried by 47 distinct reaction stable ids. Counted from the tsv: 60
Reactome rows, 14 GO_REF rows and 23 PMID rows, and all 60 Reactome rows carry aspect
cellular_component. Reactome contributed no process term at all to AP2S1. The cargo-specific pathways (Nef-mediated CD4 down-regulation,
MHC class II, WNT5A/FZD4, LDL clearance, AMPA receptor trafficking…) appear only as the
references of compartment rows.

Projection counts (QuickGO by reference, fully paginated, distinct entities counted):

  • Reactome:R-HSA-8868648 "SYNJ hydrolyze PI(4,5)P2 to PI(4)P" → 101 entities, 58 of them
    getting GO:0005829; the largest of the set is R-HSA-8868658 "HSPA8-mediated ATP
    hydrolysis promotes vesicle uncoating" at 104 entities.
    R-HSA-8868230, -8868236, -8868651, -8868658…-8871194 are the
    same shape. These are participant-compartment assignments for every protein in a CME
    reaction, not statements about AP2S1.
  • PMID:12121421 (Boll 2002, the μ2/FDNPVY paper) → 7 entities; the five AP-2 subunits
    AP2A1, AP2A2, AP2B1, AP2M1 and AP2S1 each receive the identical triple
    {GO:0030122, GO:0035615, GO:0072583} as TAS. One reference, one complex-level statement,
    five copies. The paper's experiments are entirely on μ2
    PMID:12121421.
    The conclusions are right for sigma2 anyway, but the evidence for them lives elsewhere
    (§2), so those rows are accepted while naming their real grounding.
  • The four ComplexPortal NAS references (PMID:12086608, PMID:29184887, PMID:31671891,
    PMID:15941406) give 14 entities each: the four AP-2 ComplexPortal objects
    (CPX-5149/5150/5152/5153, human and mouse α1/α2 variants) plus the five human and five
    mouse subunit accessions. Complex-level annotation distributed to components — a sound
    practice, but again one statement, not fourteen.
  • PMID:25898166 (CALM/PICALM) → 11 entities for GO:0045334.
  • PMID:10753805 (Pearse 2000 review) → 1 entity. This is the outlier: three TAS terms
    (GO:0030100, GO:0030122, GO:0048268) placed on AP2S1 alone and on no other protein in GO.
    The cached record is abstract-only, and the abstract concerns the clathrin cage, the μ2
    subunit and the α-appendage; the sigma subunit is not mentioned.
  • PMID:11102472 (SynGO) → 4 entities for GO:0048488: AP2S1, AP2B1 (human and rat) and
    AP2M1. See §8.

8. The SynGO synaptic-vesicle rows

Three rows (NAS, IDA, IMP) give GO:0048488 synaptic vesicle endocytosis from Morgan et al.
2000. The paper identifies a DLL clathrin-assembly motif and works with AP-2 purified from
bovine brain plus a peptide from the β2 subunit (examined a DLL-containing peptide from the b2 subunit of AP-2), microinjected into squid giant presynaptic terminals
PMID:11102472.
Sigma2 is never assayed separately; the assayed object is the intact complex, of which
sigma2 is an obligate subunit. That is a legitimate complex-level annotation and no reason
to overrule the curator — but it is not evidence of a sigma2-specific synaptic role, and
synaptic vesicle endocytosis is one tissue-specific instance of the general CME function
rather than the core function of a ubiquitously expressed adaptor subunit
(DR HPA; ENSG00000042753; Low tissue specificity.). Hence KEEP_AS_NON_CORE.

The same reasoning covers GO:0098884 (postsynaptic neurotransmitter receptor
internalization, ComplexPortal NAS), which has real experimental grounding on the rat
ortholog [rat P62744 carries GO:0098884 IDA/EXP from PMID:17289840, "Molecular determinants
for the interaction between AMPA receptors and the clathrin adaptor complex AP-2"], and the
two GOC term-relationship IEAs it and GO:0048488 generate (GO:0098793 presynapse from
GO:0048488; GO:0098794 postsynapse from GO:0098884).

9. The endolysosome-membrane rows — the one clear defect

Five TAS rows place AP2S1 in GO:0036020 endolysosome membrane, from
R-HSA-2130486, R-HSA-2130725, R-HSA-6784729, R-HSA-6784738 and R-HSA-8855130.
Reading the cached Reactome entries makes the mechanism plain — these are reactions in
which a clathrin-coated vesicle entity is relocated to a downstream compartment while
keeping its full subunit roster:

  • R-HSA-2130486 "Uncoating of clathrin-coated vesicles and fusion with endosomes"
  • R-HSA-6784729 "PCSK9:LDLR:Clathrin-coated vesicle transport from plasma membrane to endolysosome"
  • R-HSA-8855130 "VLDLR:PCSK9:Clathrin-coated vesicle translocates from the plasma membrane to lysosomal membrane"

The first of these is literally the step at which AP-2 leaves. Three independent lines say
the assertion is wrong:

  1. Ontology. GO's own closure places the AP-2 adaptor complex within the coated pit,
    plasma membrane and clathrin-coated endocytic vesicle (GO:0030122's ancestors include
    GO:0005905, GO:0005886, GO:0045334, GO:0030669, GO:0030666). GO:0036020 is not among
    them; its ancestry runs through lysosome and late-endosome membrane.
  2. UniProt. The record states the opposite of residence in a downstream vesicle:
    CC internalizing CCVs and to disengage from sites of endocytosis seconds
    [file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-uniprot.txt], and lists only Cell membrane and
    Membrane, coated pit as locations.
  3. Mechanism. AP-2 membrane recruitment requires PtdIns(4,5)P2, a plasma-membrane lipid;
    Reactome's own R-HSA-8868648 has synaptojanin hydrolysing it to drive uncoating.

Hence REMOVE ×5. This is a database compartment-inheritance artefact, not a curator
judgement being overruled, and no experimental annotation is touched.

By contrast GO:0030669 (clathrin-coated endocytic vesicle membrane, R-HSA-5138459
"WNT5A:FZD4 is endocytosed") and GO:0030666 (endocytic vesicle membrane, R-HSA-416639)
are kept: both are ancestors of GO:0030122 in GO's own structure, so they are entailed by
complex membership rather than contradicted by it.

10. The Pearse 2000 TAS trio

PMID:10753805 uniquely supplies GO:0030100, GO:0030122 and GO:0048268 on AP2S1.

  • GO:0030122 AP-2 adaptor complex — correct, and supported many other ways. ACCEPT.
  • GO:0048268 clathrin coat assembly — real at the complex level, but the clathrin-engaging
    element of AP-2 is the β2 hinge/clathrin box, not sigma2; sigma2 participates only as an
    obligate subunit. KEEP_AS_NON_CORE.
  • GO:0030100 regulation of endocytosis — definition "Any process that modulates the
    frequency, rate or extent of endocytosis" (QuickGO). AP-2 does not modulate CME; it is the
    cargo-selection machinery that executes it. Annotating core machinery as a regulator is
    role conflation, and the correct term, GO:0072583, is already on the gene from three
    better-grounded rows. MODIFY → GO:0072583.

11. Other biology, and what is not core

  • ARF6 / non-clathrin route. UniProt cites PubMed:19033387 for a role in maintaining
    post-endocytic trafficking through the ARF6-regulated non-clathrin pathway. No GOA row
    encodes this; it is noted but not proposed as a NEW term, because the cached record is
    abstract-only and the study is on the complex.
  • APP degradation / late-endosome-to-lysosome fusion. AP2S1 knockdown lowers APP and Aβ
    via enhanced LE-lysosome fusion, and the authors state the effect is not endocytic
    PMID:36412210.
    This is a single-laboratory finding that would, if generalised, be a second and quite
    different function. It is recorded as a knowledge gap rather than annotated: the paper
    localises APP, not AP2S1, to the late endosome, and it does not argue that sigma2 acts
    there. It is also, notably, not what the five Reactome GO:0036020 rows assert — those
    come from coated-vesicle compartment inheritance and predate the paper.
  • Behaviour. Zebrafish ap2s1 supports acoustically evoked habituation learning
    PMID:38550987.
    Non-human, complex-level, and well downstream of the molecular function; not annotated.
  • Phosphorylation. UniProt records MOD_RES 140 /note="Phosphoserine" from a
    large-scale study (PubMed:18691976). A PTM the protein carries is not an activity and
    generates no annotation.

12. What affinage missed

The affinage record is good — trust gates clear, self_evaluation_pairwise: win,
faith_pct: 100.0 — and it correctly centres the narrative on Arg15, the dileucine motif,
CaSR endocytosis and the mouse models. Two limits:

  1. It never cites the structure paper. Its citation list is
    PMID:23222959, 29479578, 33729479, 36412210, 9040778, 9767099 and one bioRxiv id
    (PMID:bio_10.1101_2024.07.22.24310683, non-numeric — a preprint, which I did not use).
    The claim it repeats from Nesbit — that Arg15 "forms key contacts with the dileucine
    motif" — traces to PMID:19140243 (Kelly et al. 2008, Nature), which affinage does
    not list. That paper is the only source for the pocket residues, the KD measurements and
    the YxxΦ specificity control, i.e. for everything in §2 and §3. The parent brief cited
    PubMed:18497731 for this structure; that PMID resolves to an unrelated FAAH gene-variant
    paper, and the correct id was found by an exact-title search on Europe PMC.
  2. It missed both assembly chaperones as mechanism. AAGAB (PMID:31353312) and CCDC32
    (PMID:42234739) are the two proteins that explain the eight protein binding IPI rows,
    and neither appears in the record. Both were found by independent Europe PMC searches on
    "AP2 dileucine" and "sigma2 dileucine" rather than on the gene symbol — the predicted
    failure mode, where the decisive paper is titled for the partner.

Its mechanism_profile GO ids (GO:0060090, GO:0038024, GO:0005768, GO:0005764) were not
imported. GO:0038024 cargo receptor activity is defined for proteins that "span
membranes"; sigma2 is a peripheral cytosolic subunit, so the id is wrong for this protein,
and the lysosome/endosome localisations are the same over-reach as §9.

13. Summary of judgements

  • Core: sigma2 is the cargo-recognition small subunit of AP-2; with the α trunk it forms the
    acidic-dileucine binding site, and through it AP-2 selects [DE]xxxL[LI]-bearing
    transmembrane cargo into clathrin-coated pits at the plasma membrane.
  • One IBA, NO_FAILURE_CORE, correct at the LCA.
  • Five REMOVEs, all the same Reactome compartment-inheritance artefact.
  • One MODIFY (regulator→executor).
  • Eight bare protein binding rows marked over-annotated, with the real activity identified
    and proposed as a new term.
  • Two NEW rows: GO:0005905 clathrin-coated pit (the defining location of AP-2, curated by
    UniProt from PubMed:33859415 and directly imaged in PMID:15941406, yet absent from GOA)
    and GO:0055074 calcium ion homeostasis (the FHH3 role, asserted by UniProt and supported
    by human genetics plus the knock-in mouse, also absent from GOA — added as a downstream
    organism-level role, deliberately not as a core function).

13a. The NEW term I decided not to add

GO:0002031 G protein-coupled receptor internalization looks like the obvious missing
term: Nesbit's experiment measures exactly that, on AP2S1, in human cells. But its GO
ancestry runs through GO:0045744 negative regulation of G protein-coupled receptor signaling pathway and GO:0009968 negative regulation of signal transduction (QuickGO
ancestor closure). Annotating AP2S1 to it would therefore assert that sigma2 negatively
regulates GPCR signalling — and the FHH3 data say the opposite direction: loss of AP2σ2
function reduces CaSR signalling, giving a rightward EC50 shift
PMID:23222959,
consistent with a separate line of work showing CaSR signals from internalized endosomal
compartments PMID:40510119.
Adding the term would import a sign-inverted regulatory claim through the ontology's own
closure. Recorded as an ONTOLOGY knowledge gap instead of annotated.

Q1. Residue identity at the claimed positions in human AP2S1

position expected observed in P53680 match role (Kelly et al. 2008, PMID:19140243)
15 R R yes basic L-4 patch with alpha R21; FHH3 mutational hotspot
63 A A yes pocket floor; A63W fills the pocket and blocks binding
65 L L yes pocket lining; L65S abolishes dileucine binding
88 V V yes pocket lining; V88D reduced binding below detection
92 N N yes pocket lining; N92W fills the pocket and blocks binding
97 N N yes contacts the L-1 position of the motif
98 V V yes pocket lining; V98S abolishes dileucine binding
103 L L yes pocket lining; L103S reduced binding below detection

All eight positions match the literature: True

Q2. Does the crystal structure's numbering transfer to the human protein?

Kelly et al. solved a species-mixed AP2 core; their Methods give the construct as
1-621 mouse alpha-adaptin; 1-591 human beta2-adaptin; 1-435 rat mu2-adaptin, 1-143 mouse sigma2-adaptin, so the sigma2 chain is the mouse protein (P62743).
The residue numbers therefore transfer to human AP2S1 only if the orthologues are
identical over these sites.

accession entry length identical to P53680 over full length residues at the 8 claimed positions
P53680 AP2S1_HUMAN 142 True RALVNNVL
P62744 AP2S1_RAT 142 True RALVNNVL
P62743 AP2S1_MOUSE 142 True RALVNNVL

Human, rat and mouse sigma2 identical over the full chain: True — so the numbering transfers without an alignment step.

id: P53680
gene_symbol: AP2S1
aliases:
- AP17
- CLAPS2
- sigma2-adaptin
- AP-2 complex subunit sigma
product_type: PROTEIN
status: COMPLETE
taxon:
  id: NCBITaxon:9606
  label: Homo sapiens
description: 'AP2S1 encodes sigma2-adaptin (AP17), the 142-residue small subunit of the heterotetrameric
  AP-2 clathrin adaptor complex, the principal cargo-selection module of clathrin-mediated endocytosis
  at the plasma membrane. AP-2 comprises two large adaptins (alpha, either AP2A1 or AP2A2, and beta2/AP2B1),
  a medium subunit (mu2/AP2M1) and sigma2, and it assembles in the cytosol from alpha/sigma2 and beta2/mu2
  hemicomplexes before being recruited to PtdIns(4,5)P2-containing membrane, where it reads sorting signals
  in the cytoplasmic tails of transmembrane cargo and links them to the clathrin lattice. The complex
  has two non-overlapping cargo sites on different subunits: mu2 reads tyrosine-based YxxPhi signals,
  while acidic dileucine signals are read by the alpha/sigma2 pair. Sigma2''s own contribution is structurally
  defined -- the two leucines of the motif insert into adjacent hydrophobic pockets built from sigma2
  residues (A63, L65, V88, N92, V98, L103, with N97 contacting the L-1 position), and sigma2 Arg15 together
  with alpha Arg21 forms the basic patch that binds the acidic residue four positions upstream. Sigma2
  thus sits on the cytoplasmic face of the plasma membrane in forming clathrin-coated pits, and its folded
  longin-like domain has no catalytic activity of its own. Loss of the subunit is incompatible with endocytosis
  and, in mouse, with embryonic development; in humans, heterozygous missense substitutions of Arg15 weaken
  recognition of the dileucine motif of the calcium-sensing receptor, reducing its internalization and
  desensitising cells to extracellular calcium, which manifests as familial hypocalciuric hypercalcaemia
  type 3. Sigma2 also serves as a binding surface during ordered assembly of the complex: the chaperones
  AAGAB and CCDC32 both engage the alpha/sigma2 hemicomplex, CCDC32 doing so through a dileucine motif
  that mimics cargo.'
