      PubMed-verified by exact-title search on Europe PMC; the AP2-core/CD4-peptide co-crystal plus
      liposome-binding mutagenesis establishes that the dileucine motif's two leucines bind pockets
      built from sigma2 residues, with a YxxPhi/PtdIns(4,5)P2 specificity control. Every residue
      claim in this review traces to it. Note that the parent task brief cited PubMed:18497731 for
      this structure; that identifier resolves to an unrelated FAAH gene-variant paper.

  publication_type: BIOINFORMATICS
  reference_review:

      chain so the rodent-structure numbering transfers exactly, and quantifies conservation across
      the eight human sigma paralogues.
- id: file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-deep-research-affinage.md
  title: Affinage mechanistic annotation for AP2S1 (human)
  publication_type: DEEP_RESEARCH
  reference_review:
    relevance: MEDIUM
    correctness: VERIFIED
    review_notes: "Machine-fetched Affinage record; trust gates clear, self_evaluation_pairwise 'win',
      faith_pct 100.0, and the record describes the correct protein (P53680, sigma2). Used as a lead only.
      Two limits recorded: it never cites PMID:19140243, the structure paper that is the sole source for
      the pocket residues it alludes to via Nesbit, and it misses both AP-2 assembly chaperones (AAGAB,
      CCDC32) that explain the eight protein-binding rows. Its citation list also contains one non-numeric
      identifier, 'PMID:bio_10.1101_2024.07.22.24310683', which is a bioRxiv preprint and was not used.
      Its mechanism_profile GO ids were not imported; GO:0038024 cargo receptor activity in particular
      is defined for proteins that span membranes, which sigma2 does not."
existing_annotations:
- term:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:25416956
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:Q6PD74
  review:
    summary: 'One of seven bare protein-binding rows citing AAGAB (Q6PD74, alpha- and gamma-adaptin-binding
      protein p34), each from a proteome-scale interactome deposition. The pairing is real and heavily
      replicated (UniProt records NbExp=12 for the IntAct interaction), but the mechanistic follow-up
      shows it is not a sigma2 activity: sigma2 does not bind AAGAB on its own, and only joins it once
      the alpha subunit is present, as part of an alpha:sigma2:AAGAB assembly intermediate.'
    action: MARK_AS_OVER_ANNOTATED
    reason: "GO:0005515 states nothing about what AP2S1 does, and here it cannot be upgraded. Resolving
      the partner shows the association is alpha-dependent rather than a direct sigma2 binding activity,
      so no 'AAGAB binding'-style informative function is warranted on this subunit. The reference-projection
      test confirms these are catalogue depositions rather than statements about sigma2: counting distinct
      gene-product entities per reference in QuickGO with full pagination gives 1570 (PMID:25416956),
      2324 (PMID:28514442), 449 (PMID:29892012), 746 (PMID:32296183), 4757 (PMID:33961781), 1394 (PMID:35271311),
      1815 (PMID:40205054). The informative molecular function that AP2S1 does have -- recognition of
      acidic dileucine sorting signals -- has no GO term to modify to; that gap is recorded under proposed_new_terms
      and knowledge_gaps rather than papered over with an ill-fitting id."
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:31353312
      supporting_text: Interestingly, while the σ2 subunit itself did not associate with AAGAB in
        co-IP, it interacted with AAGAB when the α subunit was co-expressed
    - reference_id: PMID:31353312
      supporting_text: We observed that the σ2 subunit interacted stoichiometrically with AAGAB and
        the α subunit

  qualifier: enables

  - UniProtKB:Q9BV29
  review:
    summary: "The one interaction row that is not a screen projection: only two entities in all of GOA
      cite PMID:33859415 for GO:0005515, AP2S1 and CCDC32 (Q9BV29, formerly C15orf57). UniProt's curated
      SUBUNIT line, written from the full text, records the interaction as direct and as the route by
      which CCDC32 associates with AP-2, and a subsequent structural study shows CCDC32 engaging the alpha/sigma2
      hemicomplex through a canonical dileucine cargo motif -- i.e. through sigma2's own cargo-binding
      site."
    action: MARK_AS_OVER_ANNOTATED
    reason: "The interaction is genuine and mechanistically important, but the term recorded is bare protein
      binding, which conveys none of that. The activity it stands for is sigma2's recognition of an acidic
      dileucine motif, and GO has no molecular function for that: the nearest term, GO:0089710 endocytic
      targeting sequence binding, is defined for a signal 'with an essential tyrosine (Y)', which is the
      YxxPhi motif read by the mu2 subunit, not the dileucine motif read by sigma2. With no correct replacement
      available, the honest call is to mark the bare term over-annotated and register the missing term,
      rather than MODIFY to an id whose definition excludes this ligand."
    supported_by:

- term:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  evidence_type: IPI
  original_reference_id: PMID:35271311
  qualifier: enables
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:Q6PD74
  review:
    summary: 'One of seven bare protein-binding rows citing AAGAB (Q6PD74, alpha- and gamma-adaptin-binding
      protein p34), each from a proteome-scale interactome deposition. The pairing is real and heavily
      replicated (UniProt records NbExp=12 for the IntAct interaction), but the mechanistic follow-up
      shows it is not a sigma2 activity: sigma2 does not bind AAGAB on its own, and only joins it once
      the alpha subunit is present, as part of an alpha:sigma2:AAGAB assembly intermediate.'
    action: MARK_AS_OVER_ANNOTATED
    reason: "GO:0005515 states nothing about what AP2S1 does, and here it cannot be upgraded. Resolving
      the partner shows the association is alpha-dependent rather than a direct sigma2 binding activity,
      so no 'AAGAB binding'-style informative function is warranted on this subunit. The reference-projection
      test confirms these are catalogue depositions rather than statements about sigma2: counting distinct
      gene-product entities per reference in QuickGO with full pagination gives 1570 (PMID:25416956),
      2324 (PMID:28514442), 449 (PMID:29892012), 746 (PMID:32296183), 4757 (PMID:33961781), 1394 (PMID:35271311),
      1815 (PMID:40205054). The informative molecular function that AP2S1 does have -- recognition of
      acidic dileucine sorting signals -- has no GO term to modify to; that gap is recorded under proposed_new_terms
      and knowledge_gaps rather than papered over with an ill-fitting id."
    supported_by:

  original_reference_id: Reactome:R-HSA-167537
  qualifier: located_in
  review:
    summary: One of thirty-one identical Reactome cytosol rows. The underlying biology is real --
      AP-2 is assembled in the cytosol and cycles between a soluble pool and the membrane -- but
      this row is a participant-compartment assignment attached to a Reactome reaction
      (Reactome:R-HSA-167537), not an observation about AP2S1.
    action: KEEP_AS_NON_CORE
    reason: "Keeping rather than removing, because the soluble cytosolic pool is genuine: AP-2 assembles
      in the cytosol from the alpha/sigma2 and beta2/mu2 hemicomplexes before being recruited to PtdIns(4,5)P2-containing
      membrane. Non-core, because the cytosol is where the complex waits rather than where AP2S1 does
      anything; the functionally informative locations are the clathrin-coated pit and the cytoplasmic
      face of the plasma membrane. The reference-projection test settles what this row is. Querying QuickGO
      by reference and paginating fully, the Reactome reactions behind AP2S1's cellular-component rows
      assign the same compartment to every participant of the reaction -- R-HSA-8868658 alone reaches
      104 gene products. 47 distinct Reactome reaction ids contribute AP2S1's 60 Reactome rows, and every
      one of those rows is a cellular-component row: Reactome supplies this gene no biological-process
      term at all. These are participant-compartment assignments imported wholesale, not per-gene author
      statements."

    id: GO:0005829
    label: cytosol
  evidence_type: TAS
  original_reference_id: Reactome:R-HSA-8869438
  qualifier: located_in
  review:
    summary: One of thirty-one identical Reactome cytosol rows. The underlying biology is real --
      AP-2 is assembled in the cytosol and cycles between a soluble pool and the membrane -- but
      this row is a participant-compartment assignment attached to a Reactome reaction
      (Reactome:R-HSA-8869438), not an observation about AP2S1.
    action: KEEP_AS_NON_CORE
    reason: "Keeping rather than removing, because the soluble cytosolic pool is genuine: AP-2 assembles
      in the cytosol from the alpha/sigma2 and beta2/mu2 hemicomplexes before being recruited to PtdIns(4,5)P2-containing
      membrane. Non-core, because the cytosol is where the complex waits rather than where AP2S1 does
      anything; the functionally informative locations are the clathrin-coated pit and the cytoplasmic
      face of the plasma membrane. The reference-projection test settles what this row is. Querying QuickGO
      by reference and paginating fully, the Reactome reactions behind AP2S1's cellular-component rows
      assign the same compartment to every participant of the reaction -- R-HSA-8868658 alone reaches
      104 gene products. 47 distinct Reactome reaction ids contribute AP2S1's 60 Reactome rows, and every
      one of those rows is a cellular-component row: Reactome supplies this gene no biological-process
      term at all. These are participant-compartment assignments imported wholesale, not per-gene author
      statements."

  original_reference_id: GO_REF:0000024
  qualifier: located_in
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:P63010
  review:
    summary: Plasma membrane transferred under GO_REF:0000024 with UniProtKB:P63010 as the source.
      P63010 is AP2B1, the beta-2 subunit of the same AP-2 complex -- a co-complex partner, not a
      sequence orthologue or paralogue of sigma2. UniProt uses ECO:0000250 with that accession as a
      complex-level 'inferred from' marker, and the conclusion it reaches is correct.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: 'The destination is right and is confirmed several other ways: the ComplexPortal NAS row places
      AP-2 on the cytoplasmic side of the plasma membrane, UniProt curates a coated-pit location from
      PubMed:33859415, and live imaging finds AP-2 at essentially all static clathrin spots. What is worth
      flagging is the shape of the inference rather than its outcome: an ISS whose source is a different
      subunit of the same complex is a complex-membership argument wearing sequence-similarity clothing.
      It is sound here because AP-2 is an obligate heterotetramer, but it is not the evidence the code
      implies.'

        from the disappearing population of clathrin spots

        source_status: SUPPORTS_TRANSFER
        comment: AP2B1_HUMAN, the beta-2 subunit of the same AP-2 complex -- not a sequence
          orthologue of sigma2 and not a paralogue of it. UniProt uses ECO:0000250 with this

          weak as a sequence inference. The conclusion is independently confirmed by the

          coated-pit location.

        recruited, which no residue-level argument bears on; the transfer was judged on the

        corroboration of the destination.

    summary: "The family's single PAINT inference, and it is placed correctly. Node PTN000204281 is the
      only PTN node in PTHR11753, and its GO:0016192 IBD is seeded by sigma subunits of three different
      AP complexes: the AP-3 sigmas of budding yeast and Candida (Golgi-to-vacuole transport, IMP), the
      AP-1 sigmas of yeast, fission yeast, fly and mouse (endosome-to-Golgi retrieval and synaptic-vesicle

      IMP/IDA). Human AP2S1 is inside that clade -- UniProt assigns it to PTHR11753 -- and its cargo-binding
      surface is intact, so the inference transfers cleanly."
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: "Vesicle-mediated transport is true of AP2S1 and is core: the AP-2 heterotetramer it belongs
      to is the cargo-selection module of clathrin-mediated endocytosis, and cells lacking AP2S1 fail
      to internalise cargo at all. The term is broad, but that breadth is the right answer rather than
      a defect. The node's seeds do not agree on a pathway -- AP-3 seeds act between Golgi and vacuole,
      AP-1 seeds between endosome and Golgi, AP-2 seeds at the plasma membrane -- so their last common
      ancestor supports nothing more specific than vesicle-mediated transport, and GRANULARITY_MISMATCH
      (which applies only when donors agree and a finer term was available) does not. The more specific
      human-appropriate term GO:0072583 is separately annotated three times, so nothing is lost. There
      is no IRD or IKR anywhere in this family's PAINT slice, and no target-specific evidence of loss
      or divergence: the eight residues that build sigma2's dileucine-binding site are all present in
      P53680. Bookkeeping note, recorded rather than explained: the GOA/QuickGO WITH/FROM carries eleven
      gene donors while the current local PAINT slice lists ten seeds for this term, the extra one (FB:FBgn0043012)
      appearing in the same node's GO:0043231 seed list."

- term:
    id: GO:0030100
    label: regulation of endocytosis
  evidence_type: TAS
  original_reference_id: PMID:10753805
  qualifier: involved_in
  review:
    summary: Regulation of endocytosis, a legacy UniProt TAS from a structural review of clathrin
      coat construction. Notably this reference projects to exactly one entity in all of GOA --
      AP2S1 -- and the cached record is abstract-only, its abstract discussing the clathrin cage,
      the mu2 subunit and the alpha-appendage without mentioning the sigma subunit.
    action: MODIFY
    reason: "GO:0030100 is defined as 'Any process that modulates the frequency, rate or extent of endocytosis'.
      AP-2 does not modulate clathrin-mediated endocytosis; it is the cargo-selection machinery that carries
      it out, and cargo uptake fails outright in cells deficient in AP2S1. Annotating core machinery as
      a regulator of its own process is a role conflation, and the correct term is the process itself.
      Proposing GO:0072583, which the record already carries from three better-grounded rows, so the modification
      loses no information and removes a misleading regulatory reading. MODIFY rather than REMOVE because
      the annotation is not false about the biology, only about the role: the cited review really is discussing
      AP-2 in endocytosis, and the curator's intent is recoverable as the process term. The two actions
      have the same net effect on the term set here, since GO:0072583 is already present three times;
      MODIFY is preferred because it records what the row was trying to say, which REMOVE would discard.
      If GOA would rather retire the row outright, the MISCITED mark on PMID:10753805 carries the same
      information."
    proposed_replacement_terms:
    - id: GO:0072583
      label: clathrin-dependent endocytosis
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:31353312
      supporting_text: the uptake of the cargo reporter was abolished in AAGAB KO cells or cells
        deficient in AP2S1, which encodes the σ2 subunit of AP2 adaptor
    - reference_id: PMID:23222959
      supporting_text: AP2S1 mutations decreased the sensitivity of CaSR-expressing cells to
        extracellular calcium and reduced CaSR endocytosis

    - PMID:31353312

    summary: "AP-2 adaptor complex, BHF-UCL, from Boll et al. 2002. This reference is a textbook projection:

      statement is true of sigma2, which really is one of the four chains of AP-2, and much better
      evidence exists for that elsewhere on this record (the AP2 core structure, the ARBA/FunFam
      rule, ComplexPortal). Accepted on those grounds, with the projection noted so the row is not
      mistaken for independent support.

    id: GO:0035615

  evidence_type: IEA
  original_reference_id: GO_REF:0000002

  review:
    summary: Clathrin-cargo adaptor activity mapped from IPR027156, the InterPro entry specific to
      the AP-2 sigma subunit rather than to the sigma family as a whole.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: "The activity is a property of the assembled complex rather than of sigma2 alone -- bringing
      cargo together with clathrin needs the beta2 clathrin box and the alpha trunk as well -- so this
      is a contributory annotation in substance. It is nevertheless the best molecular function GO currently
      offers for this protein, and sigma2's contribution to it is direct and structurally resolved: the
      two leucines of an acidic dileucine motif bind pockets built from sigma2 residues. Accepted, with
      the caveat recorded in core_functions as contributes_to_molecular_function rather than molecular_function."
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:19140243
      supporting_text: The major recognition events are the two leucine residues binding in
        hydrophobic pockets on σ2.

      supporting_text: binds in two adjacent hydrophobic pockets on the small σ2 subunit that are
        lined by a number of hydrophobic residues
    - reference_id: PMID:19140243
      supporting_text: Mutation of a number of these to hydrophilic residues (σ2L65S, σ2V88D, σ2V98S
        or σ2L103S), or filling in the pocket by replacement of σ2A63 or σ2N92 with tryptophan,
        strongly inhibited binding of recombinant AP2 core complexes to different dileucine motifs

  review:
    summary: Clathrin-cargo adaptor activity, BHF-UCL, from the same five-way projection. The cited

      right about the conclusion and silent about this subunit's contribution to it.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: "Kept because the conclusion is correct and because the real evidence for sigma2's part in
      it is strong, just located in a different paper: the AP2-CD4 dileucine co-crystal shows the motif's
      two leucines binding pockets on sigma2, with six pocket mutations abolishing binding while PtdIns(4,5)P2
      and YxxPhi binding are unaffected. That control is what makes the assignment subunit-specific rather
      than inherited from the complex. Graded as a contributory activity in core_functions, since generating
      a coated vesicle needs the whole adaptor."

- term:
    id: GO:0036020
    label: endolysosome membrane
  evidence_type: TAS
  original_reference_id: Reactome:R-HSA-2130486
  qualifier: located_in
  review:
    summary: "One of five Reactome rows placing AP2S1 in the endolysosome membrane, from Reactome:R-HSA-2130486.
      Reading the cached Reactome entries shows what produced them: these are reactions in which a clathrin-coated-vesicle
      entity is relocated to a downstream compartment while keeping its full subunit roster -- 'Uncoating
      of clathrin-coated vesicles and fusion with endosomes' (R-HSA-2130486), 'PCSK9:LDLR:Clathrin-coated
      vesicle transport from plasma membrane to endolysosome' (R-HSA-6784729), 'VLDLR:PCSK9:Clathrin-coated
      vesicle translocates from the plasma membrane to lysosomal membrane' (R-HSA-8855130). The first
      of those is literally the step at which AP-2 leaves."
    action: REMOVE
    reason: "Three independent lines say AP2S1 is not located in the endolysosome membrane. Ontology:
      GO's own closure places the AP-2 adaptor complex in the coated pit, the plasma membrane and the
      clathrin-coated endocytic vesicle -- GO:0005905, GO:0005886, GO:0045334, GO:0030669 and GO:0030666
      are all ancestors of GO:0030122 -- while GO:0036020 is not, its ancestry running through lysosome
      and late-endosome membrane instead. UniProt: the record lists only cell membrane and coated pit,
      and explicitly notes that AP-2 disengages before internalization. Mechanism: AP-2 membrane recruitment
      requires PtdIns(4,5)P2, a plasma-membrane lipid, and Reactome's own R-HSA-8868648 has synaptojanin
      hydrolysing it to drive uncoating. This is a pathway-database compartment-inheritance artefact,
      not a curator judgement being overruled, and no experimental annotation is touched. (It is also
      not the same claim as the reported effect of AP2S1 knockdown on late-endosome-to-lysosome fusion:
      that paper localises APP, not AP2S1, to the late endosome, and postdates these rows.)"
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: internalizing CCVs and to disengage from sites of endocytosis seconds
    - reference_id: file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-uniprot.txt
      supporting_text: Membrane, coated pit {ECO:0000269|PubMed:33859415}; Peripheral membrane
    - reference_id: PMID:15941406
      supporting_text: while AP-2 is present in nearly all static clathrin spots, it is excluded
        from the disappearing population of clathrin spots

- term:
    id: GO:0045202
    label: synapse
  evidence_type: IEA
  original_reference_id: GO_REF:0000107
  qualifier: is_active_in
  supporting_entities:
  - UniProtKB:P62743
  - ensembl:ENSMUSP00000083281
  review:
    summary: Synapse, projected from mouse Ap2s1 (P62743 / ENSMUSP00000083281) by Ensembl Compara
      orthology.
    action: KEEP_AS_NON_CORE
    reason: The orthology step is about as safe as orthology gets -- human and mouse sigma2 are
      identical over all 142 residues, verified live in the bioinformatics analysis -- so the
      projection carries whatever the mouse annotation is worth. What it is worth is a generic

- term:
    id: GO:0005905
    label: clathrin-coated pit
  evidence_type: IDA
  original_reference_id: PMID:15941406
  qualifier: located_in
  review:
    summary: "The defining location of AP-2 is absent from GOA. AP-2 is the canonical marker of clathrin-coated
      pits: live imaging finds it at essentially every static clathrin spot on the plasma membrane, and
      UniProt curates 'Membrane, coated pit' for AP2S1 from PubMed:33859415 with a peripheral, cytoplasmic-side
      topology."
    action: NEW
    reason: "GOA gives AP2S1 the plasma membrane 24 times, the cytosol 31 times and the clathrin-coated
      endocytic vesicle once, but never the coated pit -- the one structure AP-2 actually builds and occupies.
      GO's closure entails it (GO:0005905 is an ancestor of GO:0030122), which is precisely why the direct
      annotation is worth making explicit rather than leaving implicit behind a complex term. Coded IDA
      on the imaging evidence, which is of the AP-2 complex in live cells; the sigma2-specific attribution
      rests on AP-2 being an obligate heterotetramer, and UniProt makes the same attribution. A GOA curator
      may reasonably decline it as redundant-by-closure, and that objection is understood: the argument
      for making it anyway is that the coated pit is the structure AP-2 assembles and occupies, that UniProt
      curates it as an explicit subcellular location from experimental evidence, and that leaving the
      record's only direct locations as the plasma membrane and the cytosol misrepresents where the protein
      is concentrated. If the project prefers closure-implied terms to stay implicit, this is the row
      to drop; nothing else in the review depends on it."
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:15941406
      supporting_text: while AP-2 is present in nearly all static clathrin spots, it is excluded

  review:
    summary: The role that made this gene famous is missing from GOA entirely. Heterozygous Arg15
      missense mutations in AP2S1 cause familial hypocalciuric hypercalcaemia type 3, with lifelong
      elevation of serum calcium; the mutations reduce CaSR endocytosis and desensitise
      CaSR-expressing cells to extracellular calcium, and a CRISPR knock-in mouse carrying
      p.Arg15Leu reproduces the hypercalcaemia and hypermagnesaemia. UniProt records the role in its
      FUNCTION line.
    action: NEW
    reason: "Well replicated in humans and in mouse: Arg15 mutations were found in thirteen unrelated
      probands in the original series and seventeen more in the follow-up, all three alleles acting dominant-negatively
      on CaSR signal transduction, and the knock-in mouse shows the corresponding plasma chemistry. Coded
      IMP on the human genetic and cellular data rather than on the mouse, which is cited as a second
      reference. Deliberately not placed in core_functions: this is the organism-level consequence of
      sigma2's molecular job (selecting a dileucine-bearing GPCR into coated pits), not a second function
      of the protein."

core_functions:
- description: 'Acidic dileucine sorting-signal recognition within the AP-2 adaptor. Sigma2 supplies the
    two adjacent hydrophobic pockets into which the leucine pair of a [D/E]xxxL[L/I] motif inserts, and
    contributes Arg15 to the basic patch that binds the acidic residue at the L-4 position; the alpha
    trunk supplies Arg21 to the same patch. Six pocket substitutions (L65S, V88D, V98S, L103S, and the
    space-filling A63W and N92W) strongly inhibit or abolish binding of recombinant AP2 cores to dileucine
    motifs on PtdIns(4,5)P2 liposomes, while binding to PtdIns(4,5)P2 itself and to the TGN38 YxxPhi motif
    is unaffected -- the control that makes this a sigma2-specific assignment rather than one inherited
    from the complex. All eight residues are present in human P53680, and human, rat and mouse sigma2
    are identical over the full chain, so the rodent-structure numbering transfers exactly. The activity
    is recorded as a contributory one because generating a coated vesicle requires the whole adaptor:
    no GO molecular function yet exists for dileucine-signal binding itself (GO:0089710 is defined for
    tyrosine-containing signals and belongs to mu2), and that gap is proposed as a new term.'
  contributes_to_molecular_function:

- description: "Obligate structural subunit of the AP-2 heterotetramer. Sigma2 is one of the four chains
    resolved in the 2.6 A structure of the 200 kDa AP2 core, and it pairs with the alpha trunk to form
    one of the two hemicomplexes from which AP-2 is built. The subunit is not dispensable scaffolding:
    in cells deficient in AP2S1 cargo uptake is abolished, and the FHH3 Arg15Leu substitution impairs
    sigma2's interactions with the other three subunits as well as with cargo. Sigma2 is also the surface
    through which the two assembly chaperones act -- AAGAB binds it only once the alpha subunit is present,
    forming an alpha:sigma2:AAGAB intermediate, and CCDC32 engages the alpha/sigma2 hemicomplex through
    a canonical dileucine cargo motif plus two amphipathic helices."

proposed_new_terms:
- proposed_name: acidic dileucine sorting signal binding
  proposed_definition: Binding to an acidic dileucine sorting signal, a short linear motif of the
    form [D/E]xxxL[L/I] found in the cytoplasmic tails of transmembrane cargo proteins, which
    directs their selection into vesicles by heterotetrameric adaptor protein complexes.
  proposed_parent:
    id: GO:0005515
    label: protein binding
  justification: "GO can express binding of the tyrosine-based endocytic signal (GO:0089710 endocytic
    targeting sequence binding, whose definition requires 'an essential tyrosine (Y)') but has no term
    for the other major sorting signal, the acidic dileucine motif. Verified by QuickGO term lookup and
    by keyword searches for 'dileucine' (0 hits), 'sorting motif binding', 'internalization signal binding'
    and 'leucine motif', none of which returns a suitable molecular function. The gap has a direct annotation
    cost: the AP-2 sigma and alpha subunits, the AP-1 and AP-3 sigma subunits, and the GGA proteins that
    read the related DxxLL motif all lack an informative molecular function and fall back on bare protein
    binding. For AP2S1 specifically it is the reason eight GO:0005515 IPI rows cannot be upgraded, and
    the reason the disease mechanism of FHH3 -- weakened recognition of the CaSR dileucine motif by sigma2
    Arg15 -- cannot be stated in GO terms. A sibling of GO:0089710 under GO:0005515 would mirror the existing
    structure exactly."
  supported_by:
  - reference_id: PMID:19140243
    supporting_text: The major recognition events are the two leucine residues binding in
      hydrophobic pockets on σ2.
  - reference_id: PMID:19140243
    supporting_text: binds in two adjacent hydrophobic pockets on the small σ2 subunit that are
      lined by a number of hydrophobic residues
  - reference_id: PMID:19140243
    supporting_text: Mutation of a number of these to hydrophilic residues (σ2L65S, σ2V88D, σ2V98S
      or σ2L103S), or filling in the pocket by replacement of σ2A63 or σ2N92 with tryptophan,
      strongly inhibited binding of recombinant AP2 core complexes to different dileucine motifs
  - reference_id: PMID:23222959
    supporting_text: missense mutations of AP2 σ subunit (AP2S1) affecting Arg15, which forms key
      contacts with dileucine-based motifs of CCV cargo proteins, result in familial hypocalciuric
      hypercalcemia type 3 (FHH3), an extracellular calcium homeostasis disorder affecting the
      parathyroids, kidneys and bone
knowledge_gaps:
- gap_statement: 'GO cannot express the one molecular activity that is specific to sigma2 rather than
    to the AP-2 complex: binding of the acidic dileucine sorting signal.'
  boundary: "Firmly established: the dileucine motif's two leucines bind adjacent hydrophobic pockets
    built from sigma2 residues; six pocket substitutions abolish or strongly reduce binding while leaving
    PtdIns(4,5)P2 and YxxPhi binding intact; sigma2 Arg15 with alpha Arg21 binds the L-4 residue; and
    all of these residues are present in the human protein. Equally firmly established on the ontology
    side: GO:0089710, the only endocytic-signal binding term, is defined for a signal with an essential
    tyrosine, which is the mu2 ligand."
  gap_kind:
  - ONTOLOGY
  status: OPEN
  significance: Without the term, sigma2's function is representable in GO only as the complex-level
    GO:0035615 or as bare GO:0005515, and the molecular mechanism of a Mendelian disease (FHH3) has
    no GO expression. The same gap affects the alpha, AP-1 sigma, AP-3 sigma and GGA proteins.
  resolution: Create a sibling of GO:0089710 under GO:0005515 for acidic dileucine sorting signal
    binding, as set out in proposed_new_terms; then the eight protein-binding rows on this gene
    become candidates for MODIFY rather than over-annotation.
  provenance:
  - reference_id: PMID:19140243
    supporting_text: The major recognition events are the two leucine residues binding in
      hydrophobic pockets on σ2.
  - reference_id: PMID:19140243
    supporting_text: Mutation of a number of these to hydrophilic residues (σ2L65S, σ2V88D, σ2V98S
      or σ2L103S), or filling in the pocket by replacement of σ2A63 or σ2N92 with tryptophan,
      strongly inhibited binding of recombinant AP2 core complexes to different dileucine motifs
- gap_statement: The obvious GO term for AP2S1's best-documented cargo function, GO:0002031 G
    protein-coupled receptor internalization, cannot be used because its ontological ancestry
    asserts a regulatory sign the data contradict.
  boundary: "Established: AP2S1 Arg15 mutants reduce CaSR internalization and shift the calcium concentration-response
    curve rightward, so loss of sigma2 function reduces CaSR signalling. Established on the ontology side:
    GO:0002031's is_a/part_of closure includes GO:0045744 negative regulation of G protein-coupled receptor
    signaling pathway and GO:0009968 negative regulation of signal transduction."
  gap_kind:
  - ONTOLOGY
  - BIOLOGY
  status: OPEN
  significance: Annotating the term would propagate a claim that sigma2 negatively regulates GPCR
    signalling, when the human genetics show the opposite direction for this receptor -- consistent
    with work showing CaSR signals from internalized endosomal compartments, so that internalization
    sustains rather than terminates signalling. The row is therefore left unmade and recorded here.
  resolution: Either loosen the regulatory parentage of GO:0002031 so that receptor internalization
    does not entail negative regulation of the receptor's pathway, or determine experimentally
    whether CaSR is the exception among AP-2 GPCR cargo. Both are tractable.
  provenance:
  - reference_id: PMID:23222959
    supporting_text: This revealed that expression of all three mutant AP2σ2s led to a rightward
      shift in the concentration–response curves
  - reference_id: PMID:40510119
    supporting_text: From early endosomes, internalized CaSR is targeted to spatially distinct
      pathways involving early-to-recycling and late endosomes, to direct compartment-specific
      signals and regulate GPCR forward trafficking.
- gap_statement: Whether AP-2 is a genuine component of the budded clathrin-coated vesicle, or only
    of the coated pit, is unresolved, and the two best experiments disagree.
  boundary: 'Established on one side: CCV proteomics places AP2 among the three most abundant proteins
    of mammalian endocytic clathrin-coated vesicles. Established on the other: quantitative live imaging
    of at least 80 clathrin spots per group finds AP-2 at nearly all static spots but excluded from the
    disappearing (internalizing) population, and UniProt records the same, noting that AP-2 disengages
    seconds before internalization.'
  gap_kind:
  - BIOLOGY
  - CURATION
  status: OPEN
  significance: Four cellular-component terms on this gene (GO:0045334, GO:0030669, GO:0030666, and
    by entailment part of GO:0030122's own placement in the ontology) depend on the answer. They are
    accepted or kept here on the ontology's own structure rather than on a resolution of the
    biology, and the distinction matters for any downstream use that reads AP-2 as a cargo-carrying
    vesicle protein.
  resolution: 'The two results are not strictly incompatible: proteomics measures a population purified
    over minutes, imaging measures individual events at the second scale, and residual AP-2 below the
    imaging threshold would satisfy both. A direct test would be single-vesicle correlative imaging of
    scission events with calibrated sigma2 copy number.'
  provenance:
  - reference_id: PMID:25898166
    supporting_text: The three most abundant proteins in mammalian endocytic CCVs are clathrin and
      the two cargo-selecting, clathrin adaptors, CALM and AP2.
  - reference_id: PMID:15941406
    supporting_text: while AP-2 is present in nearly all static clathrin spots, it is excluded from
      the disappearing population of clathrin spots
  - reference_id: file:human/AP2S1/AP2S1-uniprot.txt
    supporting_text: internalizing CCVs and to disengage from sites of endocytosis seconds
- gap_statement: The reported endocytosis-independent role of AP2S1 in amyloid precursor protein
    turnover, acting through late-endosome-to-lysosome fusion, rests on a single laboratory and has
    no mechanistic link to sigma2's known activity.
  boundary: 'Reported: AP2S1 knockdown or overexpression decreases or increases APP and Abeta; the effect
    is stated to be unrelated to endocytosis and to require VPS41; hippocampal AAV knockdown in APP/PS1
    mice lowers APP and Abeta and improves cognition. Not reported: any localisation of AP2S1 itself to
    the late endosome, or any biochemical connection between the dileucine pocket and the fusion machinery.'
  gap_kind:
  - BIOLOGY
  status: OPEN
  significance: If confirmed and shown to be direct, this would be a second, mechanistically
    separate function for the subunit and would warrant its own annotations. As it stands, an
    indirect consequence of perturbing a complex on which most membrane traffic depends is at least
    as likely. No annotation is made from it here.
  resolution: Independent replication, plus localisation of endogenous AP2S1 (not APP) to
    RAB9-positive late endosomes, and a separation-of-function allele that preserves endocytosis
    while losing the APP effect.
  provenance:
  - reference_id: PMID:36412210
    supporting_text: This effect was unrelated to endocytosis but involved lysosomal degradation.
- gap_statement: Why complete loss of AP2S1 is embryonic-lethal and blocks endocytosis generally,
    while the human Arg15 alleles produce only a narrow calcium-homeostasis disorder, is not
    explained.
  boundary: 'Established: homozygous Ap2s1 loss-of-function mice die between E3.5 and E9.5 and homozygous
    Arg15Leu knock-ins die perinatally, while heterozygotes are haplosufficient; human Arg15 heterozygotes
    have lifelong hypercalcaemia and are otherwise largely asymptomatic. Established mechanistically:
    Arg15 contributes only to the basic L-4 patch, not to the leucine pockets themselves, and its double
    mutation with alpha Arg21 costs about an order of magnitude in affinity rather than abolishing binding.'
  gap_kind:
  - BIOLOGY
  status: OPEN
  significance: The original authors proposed that Arg15 is specifically required for recognition of
    the CaSR motif. If true, the L-4 patch encodes motif selectivity rather than affinity alone,
    which would be a general result about how AP complexes discriminate between dileucine cargoes.
  resolution: Compare AP-2 Arg15 mutant binding across a panel of dileucine motifs differing at the
    L-4 position (the CaSR motif RHQPLL has a non-acidic L-4), and measure surface levels of several
    dileucine cargoes in Arg15 knock-in cells.
  provenance:
  - reference_id: PMID:19140243
    supporting_text: A double mutant in σ2R15 and αR21 decreased binding to the dileucine motif by
      around an order of magnitude
  - reference_id: PMID:23222959
    supporting_text: missense mutations of AP2 σ subunit (AP2S1) affecting Arg15, which forms key
      contacts with dileucine-based motifs of CCV cargo proteins, result in familial hypocalciuric
      hypercalcemia type 3 (FHH3), an extracellular calcium homeostasis disorder affecting the
      parathyroids, kidneys and bone
  - reference_id: PMID:29479578
    supporting_text: However, homozygous Ap2s1del17/del17 mice were non-viable and died between
      embryonic days 3.5 and 9.5
suggested_questions:
- question: Should GO create a molecular function for acidic dileucine ([D/E]xxxL[L/I]) sorting
    signal binding as a sibling of GO:0089710? Without it, the AP-2 alpha and sigma subunits, the
    AP-1 and AP-3 sigmas and the GGA proteins all fall back on bare protein binding, and the
    molecular mechanism of FHH3 has no GO expression.
  experts:
  - GO Ontology editors
  - Gene Ontology membrane-trafficking curators
- question: GO:0002031 G protein-coupled receptor internalization has negative regulation of GPCR
    signalling in its ancestry. Is that entailment intended? AP-2-mediated CaSR internalization
    sustains rather than terminates signalling, so the term cannot be used for the
    best-characterised cargo of AP2S1.
  experts:
  - GO Ontology editors
  - GPCR trafficking curators
- question: Should the five Reactome-derived GO:0036020 endolysosome membrane rows on AP2S1 be
    corrected at source? They arise because a clathrin-coated-vesicle entity keeps its full subunit
    roster while being relocated, including in a reaction named 'Uncoating of clathrin-coated
    vesicles and fusion with endosomes'. The same pattern will affect every AP-2 subunit and every
    clathrin chain.
  experts:
  - Reactome curators
  - GOA
- question: Does sigma2 Arg15 confer selectivity for particular dileucine motifs rather than general
    affinity? The CaSR motif RHQPLL has a non-acidic residue at L-4, and the FHH3 phenotype is far
    narrower than the endocytic defect expected from a general loss of dileucine recognition.
  experts:
  - AP complex structural biologists
  - Calcium homeostasis clinicians
- question: N92 is the one dileucine-pocket residue that distinguishes AP2S1 from every other human
    AP-1 to AP-4 sigma subunit, all of which carry D. Does it account for AP-2's reported tolerance
    of variation flanking the L[LI] pair compared with AP-1 and AP-3?
  experts:
  - AP complex structural biologists
suggested_experiments:
- hypothesis: Sigma2 Arg15 sets selectivity among dileucine motifs rather than global affinity,
    which is why FHH3 is a calcium disorder rather than a general endocytic disease.
  description: Measure, by surface plasmon resonance or liposome-binding assay, the affinity of
    recombinant wild-type and Arg15Cys/His/Leu AP2 cores for a panel of dileucine motifs
    systematically varied at the L-4 position (acidic E/D, the CaSR-like polar Q/H, and the basic R
    of GLUT4), with a TGN38 YxxPhi motif and PtdIns(4,5)P2 as internal specificity controls exactly
    as in the original structural study. A selectivity model predicts that the Arg15 mutants lose
    most affinity for non-acidic L-4 motifs while retaining it for acidic ones.
  experiment_type: biophysical binding assay
- hypothesis: N92, the single AP2-specific pocket residue, accounts for AP-2's broader dileucine
    tolerance relative to AP-1 and AP-3.
  description: 'Make the reciprocal swap: sigma2 N92D in AP-2 cores and the corresponding D-to-N substitution
    in an AP-1 core, then compare binding across a matched motif panel. Pair with a cellular readout by
    knock-in of N92D at the endogenous AP2S1 locus and quantitative surface proteomics of dileucine-bearing
    cargo.'
  experiment_type: structure-function mutagenesis
- hypothesis: AP-2 is largely absent from the budded clathrin-coated vesicle, and its presence in
    CCV proteomes reflects co-purification of pits and partially uncoated carriers.
  description: Correlative live-cell TIRF and cryo-electron tomography of scission events in cells
    expressing endogenously tagged AP2S1 at known copy number, counting sigma2 molecules per vesicle
    immediately before and after scission. This would settle which of GO:0045334, GO:0030669 and
    GO:0005905 AP2S1 should carry.
  experiment_type: quantitative imaging
- hypothesis: The reported effect of AP2S1 on APP degradation is an indirect consequence of impaired
    endocytosis rather than a distinct late-endosomal function.
  description: Repeat the APP and Abeta measurements in cells carrying a separation-of-function
    AP2S1 allele -- for example the pocket mutant V88D, which abolishes dileucine binding but leaves
    the subunit folded and complex-competent -- alongside full knockdown, and localise endogenous
    AP2S1 (not APP) relative to RAB9 and LAMP1. If the APP phenotype tracks dileucine-binding loss
    it is endocytic; if it survives V88D it is not.
  experiment_type: genetic separation of function
